\mathds{E}[\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A)]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}N^{nD-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\tau}}}[A]\;.

(89)

If (
85
) were to hold for another
coefficient
F_{\bm{\sigma}}
, one would obtain (
89
)
with
\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{\tau}}}
replaced by
F_{\bm{\tau}}
. But for
n\leq N
,
the inversion formula (
49
) imposes

\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{\tau}}}=F_{\bm{\tau}}
.
∎

One may also see the unicity of the expansion in the trace-invariants
associated to elements of the quotients
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{{\rm m}}
and

\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{{\rm p}}
from the following formulae, stated in

[
CGL25
, Proposition 4.11]
. For each
c\in\{1,\ldots D\}
,
choose
\sigma_{c}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, and distinct
1\leq j_{c}(1),\ldots,j_{c}(n)\leq N
,
then the finite size precursors
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
may be expressed
in terms of the joint cumulants of entries of the tensors with
distinct indices as:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=k_{n}\left(\left\{A_{\bm{j}\circ\bm{\sigma}(k)\;;\;\bm{j}(k)}\right\}_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)\;,\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=k_{n}\left(\left\{T_{\bm{j}({\bm{\sigma}}(k)},\bar{T}_{\bm{j}(k)}\right\}_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)\;,

(90)

and in the same way,
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
may be expressed in terms of the joint moments of the entries of the tensors with distinct indices as:

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\prod_{k=1}^{n}A_{\bm{j}\circ\bm{\sigma}(k)\;;\;\bm{j}(k)}\Bigr]\;,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\prod_{k=1}^{n}T_{\bm{j}\circ\bm{\sigma}(k)}\bar{T}_{\bm{j}(k)}\Bigr]\;.

(91)

3
Random tensors with coarser local unitary invariances

Prop.
2.19
shows that the joint moments of the tensor components can be computed from the macroscopic moments, and that for every
D
, the coefficients
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
in these expressions are unique up to relabeling. If the random tensor has a larger LU invariance group, then the same will be true for the macroscopic moments of the larger LU-invariance group. This drastically constrains the possible values that
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
and
{\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
can take and entirely solves the question of determining the tensorial free cumulants of unitarily invariant random matrices or unitarily invariant random vectors with subdivided index sets. A consequence of these drastic simplifications is that for all random tensors invariant under global unitary transformations (which implies the local unitary invariance), the
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}
are expressed through expansions over a single permutation. This implies that such distributions have the same scaling behavior, for instance.

3.1
Coarser invariance and the vanishing of finite size precursors

Consider
D^{\prime}\leq D
and a surjective mapping
\xi\colon[D]\to[D^{\prime}]
. Such a mapping defines a partition
\ker\xi
given by

\ker\xi=\Bigl\{\xi^{-1}(\{c^{\prime}\})\colon 1\leq c^{\prime}\leq D^{\prime}\Bigr\}.

(92)

The mapping
\xi
and the partition
\ker\xi
prescribe a grouping of the
D
colors in
D^{\prime}
blocks. We denote by
(1_{D})
the mapping sending
[D]
to
\{1\}
.

Consider
A
and
(T,\bar{T})
some tensors with
D
inputs each taking value in
[N]
:
A=\{A_{\bm{i};\bm{j}}\}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in[N]^{D}}
and
T=\{T_{\bm{i}}\}_{\bm{i}\in[N]^{D}}
. Consider as well a tensor
M=\{M_{\bm{i^{\prime}};\bm{j^{\prime}}}\}_{\{i^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},j^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}\in[N_{c^{\prime}}]\}_{1\leq c^{\prime}\leq D^{\prime}}}
with
D^{\prime}
inputs but the same total number of entries:
N_{c^{\prime}}=N^{p_{c^{\prime}}}
, where letting
p_{c^{\prime}}=\#\xi^{-1}(\{c^{\prime}\})
is the number of colors sent to
c^{\prime}
by
\xi
.

For each
c^{\prime}
, consider a bijective map
f_{c^{\prime}}:[N]^{p_{c^{\prime}}}\rightarrow[N_{c^{\prime}}]
, and the bijective map

f_{\xi}:[N]^{D}\simeq[N]^{p_{1}}\times\cdots\times[N]^{p_{D^{\prime}}}\rightarrow[N_{1}]\times\cdots\times[N_{D^{\prime}}]

induced by the
\{f_{c^{\prime}}\}
. If
A
is a tensor with
D
inputs each taking value in
[N]
, we let
A_{\xi}
be the tensor with
D^{\prime}
inputs obtained reorganizing the entries of
A
as
(A_{\xi})_{\bm{i^{\prime}};\bm{j^{\prime}}}=A_{f_{\xi}^{-1}(\bm{i^{\prime}});f_{\xi}^{-1}(\bm{j^{\prime}})}
. Note that
A_{\xi}
depends on the choice of bijections
f_{c}
. If for instance
\ker\xi=1_{D}
and if
f_{1_{D}}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{D})=i
and
f_{1_{D}}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})=j
, for some
i_{1},\ldots,i_{D},j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}\in[N]
and some
i,j\in[N^{D}]
, then
A_{(1_{D})}\in\mathbb{C}^{N^{D}\times N^{D}}
, and:

(A_{(1_{D})})_{i;j}=A_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{D};j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}}\;.

Reciprocally, if
M
is a tensor with
D^{\prime}
inputs, such that the input with color
c^{\prime}
takes value in
[N_{c^{\prime}}]
, we can reorganize its entries by subdividing its indices:
(M_{\xi^{-1}})_{\bm{i};\bm{j}}=M_{f_{\xi}(\bm{i});f_{\xi}(\bm{j})}
.

With these notations, we may then define the multiplication of
M
and
A
as:

(MA)_{\bm{i^{\prime}};\bm{j^{\prime}}}:=(MA_{\xi})_{\bm{i^{\prime}};\bm{j^{\prime}}}=\sum_{\bm{a^{\prime}}\in[N_{1}]\times\cdots\times[N_{D^{\prime}}]}M_{\bm{i^{\prime}};\bm{a^{\prime}}}A_{f_{\xi}^{-1}(\bm{a^{\prime}});f_{\xi}^{-1}(\bm{j^{\prime}})}\;,

(93)

and similarly for
AM
and for
MT
.

If
A
(respectively
(T,\bar{T})
) is a mixed (respectively pure) random tensor with
D
inputs, we say that it is
LU-invariant with respect to
\xi
if for any
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D^{\prime}}
,
U_{c^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{U}(N_{c^{\prime}})
,
UAU^{\dagger}
and
A
have the same distribution (respectively
(UT,\bar{U}\bar{T})
). We say that
A
(respectively
(T,\bar{T})
) is
global unitary invariant
if this holds for
\xi=1_{D}
. If a random tensor is LU-invariant for
\xi\colon[D]\to[D^{\prime}]
, then it is LU-invariant for any
\xi^{\prime}
with a finer kernel
\ker\xi^{\prime}\leq\ker\xi
.

The following map canonically defines a one-to-one correspondence between
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}
and the following subset of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

g_{\xi}:{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}})\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}\qquad\mapsto\qquad{\bm{\sigma}}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{\xi(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{\xi(D)})\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\;.

(94)

This corresponds to adding copies of the
\sigma^{\prime}_{c}
’s as prescribed by
\xi
. For instance, if
\xi=(1_{D})
, then for
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
,
g_{1_{D}}(\eta)=(\eta,\ldots,\eta)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
.

If
A
(respectively
(T,\bar{T})
) is LU-invariant with respect to
\xi\colon[D]\to[D^{\prime}]
, then the macroscopic moments describing the distribution seen with respect to this invariance are identified by
{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}
, up to
\sim_{{\rm m}}
and
\sim_{{\rm p}}
, and correspond to (see (
21
)):

\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}(A):=\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}(A_{\xi})=\Tr_{g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}(A)\;,

(95)

and similarly for
(T,\bar{T})
. We define
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[A]
,
\mathcal{K}^{\rm m}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[A]
,
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[T,\bar{T}]
,
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[T,\bar{T}]
in the same way.

If for instance
\xi=(1_{D})
and
\gamma=(1\,2\,\cdots\,n)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, then
A_{({1_{D}})}\in\mathbb{C}^{N^{D}\times N^{D}}
,
g_{(1_{D})}(\gamma)=(\gamma,\ldots,\gamma)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and one has:

\Tr(A^n)=\Tr_{\gamma}(A):=\Tr(A_{(1_D)}^n)=\Tr_{\gamma}(A_{(1_{D})})=\Tr_{(\gamma,\ldots,\gamma)}(A)\;.

(96)

If for instance
A
is a
N^{D}\times N^{D}
Wishart random matrix, then
\mathds{E}[\Tr(A^n)]=N^{D}\mathrm{Cat}_{n}(1+o(1))
and
\mathcal{K}^{\rm m}_{\gamma}[A]=N^{D}(1+o(1))
.

Remark 3.1
.

If
A
is LU-invariant for
\xi
then the law of
A_{\xi}
does not depend on the choice of
f_{1},\ldots,f_{D^{\prime}}
. Indeed, the groups
\mathbb{U}(N_{c^{\prime}}),1\leq c^{\prime}\leq D^{\prime}
contain permutation matrices of size
N_{c^{\prime}}\times N_{c^{\prime}}
, and any two choices of
f_{c^{\prime}}
are related by such a permutation.

Theorem 3.2
.

Consider a random tensor
A
(respectively
(T,\bar{T})
) with
D
inputs, and which is LU-invariant with respect to
\xi\colon[D]\to[D^{\prime}]
, then for any
n\leq N
and any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}}\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}^{\rm m}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[A_{\xi}]\;,

(97)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}}\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[T_{\xi},\bar{T}_{\xi}]\;,

(98)

where
\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\delta_{\sigma_{c},\sigma_{\xi(c)}}
. As a consequence, the same relations (
97
) relate the tensorial free cumulants
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}(a),{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}
, and
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)
,
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and similarly in the pure case.

Proof.

From the unicity of Prop.
2.19
, one has for
\bm{j}\colon[n]\to[N_{1}]\times\cdots\times[N_{D^{\prime}}]
with distinct indices:

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[A_{\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{i}}\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[(A_{\xi})_{f_{\xi}\circ(\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma});f_{\xi}\circ\bm{i}}\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}}\delta_{f_{\xi}\circ(\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma}),(f_{\xi}\circ\bm{i})\circ\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}}[A_{\xi}],

and

\delta_{f_{\xi}\circ(\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma}),(f_{\xi}\circ\bm{i})\circ\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}}=\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{c=1}^{D^{\prime}}\delta_{f{c^{\prime}}(\bm{i}_{\xi^{-1}(c^{\prime})}(\sigma_{\xi^{-1}(c^{\prime})}(k))),f_{c^{\prime}}(\bm{i}_{\xi^{-1}(c)}(\sigma^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}))}=\prod_{c=1}^{D^{\prime}}\delta_{\sigma_{\xi^{-1}(c^{\prime})},\sigma^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}}=\delta_{\sigma,g_{\xi}(\sigma^{\prime})}.

Thus, for all
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
and
\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)
with
\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})\leq\pi
:

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]=\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}}\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[A]\quad\text{ and }\quad\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\pi,{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}\\
\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},g_{\xi}({\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime})}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\pi,{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}}[A]

and similarly for the pure case. The formulae in the statement follow from the definitions of
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}
and
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}
.
∎

With these notations, the macroscopic moments and cumulants (
72
) and (
74
) of
A
or
T,\bar{T}
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D^{\prime}}}N^{nD-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime})}\sum_{\pi\geq\Pi({\bm{\tau}}^{\prime})}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime}}[A]\;,

(99)

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime}\in S^{D^{\prime}}_{n}}N^{nD-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\geq\Pi({\bm{\tau}}^{\prime})}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\pi=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}^{\prime}}[A]\;,

(100)

and similarly in the pure case.

3.2
The mixed and global unitary invariant case

Macroscopic moments and cumulants.

Lemma 3.3
.

For a
global unitary invariant
tensor
A
, the finite size precursors of the free cumulants of
A_{(1_{D})}
determine the macroscopic moments and cumulants of
A
:

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})}\sum_{\pi\geq\Pi(\eta)}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,\eta}[A]\;,

(101)

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\geq\Pi(\eta)}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\pi=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,\eta}[A]\;,

(102)

This results shows that the finite size moments and cumulants of any global unitary mixed random tensor resemble the ones of the the Wishart tensors (
39
), in the sense that they involve an expansion over a single permutation
\eta
, but with a modified weight given by the finite
N
precursors of the free cumulants.

For the Wishart tensor
W
of parameters
(N^{D},N^{D^{\prime}})
,
we identify for
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\eta}[W]=N^{D^{\prime}-n(D+D^{\prime}-1)}\delta_{K(\eta),1}\;,

(103)

for which the rightmost sum in (
101
) is one (for
\pi=\Pi(\eta)
), and the rightmost sum in (
102
) is
\delta_{K({\bm{\sigma}},\eta),1}
, so that one recovers (
39
). The rescaled limit is given by

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\eta}(w)=\delta_{K(\eta),1}\;,

(104)

in accordance with (6.46) of
[
CGL25
]
for
D^{\prime}=1
.

Asymptotics.

For
D=1
, if
\bm{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})
is a unitarily invariant family of
N\times N
random matrices in classical random matrix ensembles (GUE, Wishart with parameters of the same order, etc), then the expected scaling behavior for the joint cumulants
\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}[\bm{A}]
(up to a global rescaling of the components of the matrices) is:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}[\bm{A}]=N^{2-\#(\sigma)}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(\bm{a})+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

(105)

Furthermore, it has been shown
[
Col+07
]
that the finite size precursors of the free cumulants of a unitarily invariant random matrix (from classical ensembles) scale as:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}[A]=N^{2-\#(\sigma)-n}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

(106)

We have seen in Sec.
2.6
that the classical cumulants of the Wishart tensors
W
of parameters
(N^{D},N^{D})
scale as
N^{n-\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D})}
,
where as shown in Prop.
2.8
, the scaling can be expressed as:

n-\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D})=2D(1-K({\bm{\sigma}}))+\#{\bm{\sigma}}-n(D-1).

The same scaling determines an upper bound on the scales of the classical cumulants for any global unitary invariant random tensors that scales as (
105
) (with
N
replaced by
N^{D}
), as we now show. From (
47
), this scaling is sharp for the Wishart random tensor of parameters
(N^{D},N^{D})
. We recall the notation:

\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D}=({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{\bf id})=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{D},\mathrm{id}_{n},\ldots,\mathrm{id}_{n})\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{2D}:

The following theorem extends Prop. 6.2 of
[
NP25
]
. Their results only hold for the asymptotic moments, corresponding to the
connected

{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, while our theorem stand for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
.

Theorem 3.4
.

Consider a mixed global unitary invariant random tensor
A
with
D\geq 1
inputs such that
N^{D-1}A
scales as (
105
). Then for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
n\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{n-\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(107)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{n-\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D})-nD}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(108)

where the tensorial free cumulants of
A
coincide with its (matricial) free cumulants:

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(a)&\textrm{ if }{\bm{\sigma}}=(\sigma,\ldots,\sigma)\textrm{ for some }\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.\;.

(109)

The asymptotic cumulants of
A
are computed from the free cumulants as:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{K({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1}\\
{d(\hat{\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D})}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\eta),\eta}(a)\;.

(110)

Proof.

Starting from (
102
), one sees from (
106
) that the term corresponding to
\pi
in the rightmost sum scales as
N^{2D\#(\pi)-D\#(\eta)-nD-n(D-1)}
, so that the term that dominates the sum over
\pi\geq\Pi(\eta)
is
\pi=\Pi(\eta)
. This imposes the condition
\Pi({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1_{n}
:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{K({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1}\end{subarray}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})+D\#(\eta)-2nD+n}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\eta),\eta}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;.

Writing the exponent of
N
as
n-d(\hat{\bm{\sigma}},\eta)
allows deducing (
107
) and (
110
). The relations (
108
) and (
109
) follow from (
97
).
∎

With the same assumptions, we define the rescaled trace of
A
as:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}(a)=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\mathrm{id}_{1}}(a)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr(A)\bigr]\;.

(111)

Corollary 3.5
.

Consider a mixed global unitary invariant random tensor
A
with
D\geq 1
inputs such that
N^{D-1}A
scales sharply as (
105
). Then for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with
K({\bm{\sigma}})
fixed to
K
, the
\Phi_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\rm m}
that have the strongest scale
N^{1-(2D-1)(K-1)}
are the melonic
{\bm{\sigma}}
with canonical pairing the identity. Furthermore, if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic with
K({\bm{\sigma}})=1
, then:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=(\varphi^{{\rm m}}(a))^{n}\;.

Proof.

The first statement is a direct consequence of Cor.
2.7
for
D^{\prime}=D
. The second statement results from the fact that there is a unique
\eta
such that
d(\hat{\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}})
, corresponding to the canonical pairing of
\hat{\bm{\sigma}}
, that is,
\eta=\mathrm{id}_{n}
. From (
110
), one has therefore

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\mathrm{id}_{1}}(a))^{n}
, and from (
110
) again for
n=1
:
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\mathrm{id}_{1}}(a)=\varphi^{{\rm m}}(a)
. ∎

Remark 3.6
.

One can adapt the theorem above to include Wishart random matrices whose parameters are not of the same order. For parameters
(N^{D},N^{D^{\prime}})
, (
105
) and (
106
) must be modified to (see
[
Lio18
]
and (
103
))

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{D+D^{\prime}-D\#(\sigma)}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{D+D^{\prime}-D\#(\sigma)-nD}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

which are indeed the scaling satisfied by
N^{D^{\prime}-1}W
, where
W
is a Wishart random matrix of parameters
(N^{D},N^{D^{\prime}})
. For a global unitary invariant random tensor satisfying these assumptions, (
109
) still holds, and (
107
) and (
108
) are modified to:

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{n-\delta(\hat{\bm{\sigma}}_{D^{\prime}})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]

\displaystyle=N^{D^{\prime}-D(K({\bm{\sigma}})-1)-n(D+D^{\prime}-1)}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;.

3.3
Universality in the pure and global unitary invariant case

Macroscopic moments and cumulants.

Lemma 3.7
.

For a
global unitary invariant
pure random tensor
T
, the finite size precursors of the free cumulants of the random vector
T_{f_{1_{D}}}
determine the macroscopic moments and cumulants of the tensor
T
:

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(T,\bar{T})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})}\sum_{\Pi\geq\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}(\eta)}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\eta}[T,\bar{T}]\;,

(112)

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\Pi\geq\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}(\eta)}\\
{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}}\end{subarray}}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\eta}[T,\bar{T}]\;.

(113)

Here again, it shows that the finite size moments and cumulants of any global unitary pure random tensor resemble the ones of the complex Gaussian (
37
), but with a modified weight given by the finite
N
precursors of the free cumulants.

Asymptotics.

For
D=1
, if
T\in\mathbb{C}^{N^{D}}
is a unitarily invariant random vector, then
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\sigma}[T,\bar{T}]=\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]
since it is a class function for
\sim_{\rm p}
. Up to a global rescaling in
N
of the components of the vector, if there is no conditioning on the norm of
T
, then we expect the cumulants
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]
to scale as:

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{D}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(114)

Lemma 3.8
.

Under this assumption, one has the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]

\displaystyle=N^{D(1-n)}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(115)

\displaystyle\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}(t,\bar{t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\succeq\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\tau)\\
\#(\Pi)=n+1-\#(\tau)\\
\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})=1_{n,\bar{n}}\end{subarray}}\ \varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\tau}(t,\bar{t})\ \Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})_{[n]},\tau]\;.

(116)

Proof.

Starting from (
78
) for
D=1
, and taking into account the scaling assumption (
114
) and the asymptotics of the cumulant Weingarten function (
54
), one has:

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]&=\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\tau)\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}N^{D\#\Pi+D\#(\tau)-2D(\#\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})-1)-2nD}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\tau}(t,\bar{t})\Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})_{[n]},\tau]+o(1)\Bigr)\;,\end{split}

(117)

Using (
13
) one may therefore rewrite the exponent of
N
as

\displaystyle-DL\bigl[\Pi,\Pi_{\rm p}(\tau,\mathrm{id}_{n});[n]\bigr]-D(\#\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})-1)+D(1-n)\;,\ \mathrm{where:}

(118)

\displaystyle L\Bigl[\Pi,\Pi_{\rm p}(\tau,\mathrm{id}_{n});[n]\Bigr]=n-\#(\Pi)-\#(\tau)+\#(\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n}))\geq 0\;.

(119)

The terms that dominate the sum over
\Pi
are for
\Pi=\Pi_{\rm p}(\tau)
satisfying
\Pi_{\rm p}(\tau,\mathrm{id}_{n})=\Pi(\tau)=1_{n}

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{D(1-n)}\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\succeq\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\tau)\\
\#(\Pi)=n+1-\#(\tau)\\
\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})=1_{n,\bar{n}}\end{subarray}}\ \Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\tau}(t,\bar{t})\Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\mathrm{id}_{n})_{[n]},\tau]+o(1)\Bigr)\;,

(120)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Similarly to (
121
), we define – under this assumption – the rescaled norm of
T
:

\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})=\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{1}}(t,\bar{t})=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathds{E}\bigl[\lVert T\rVert^{2}\bigr]\;.

(121)

Theorem 3.9
.

Consider a pure global unitary invariant random tensor
T\in\mathbb{C}^{N^{D}}
and such that the pure tensor
(N^{D-1}T,N^{D-1}\bar{T})
scales as (
114
). Then for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
n\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]

\displaystyle=N^{n-\delta({\bm{\sigma}})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(122)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\rm p}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]

\displaystyle=N^{D-n(2D-1)}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(123)

where the tensorial free cumulants of
A
coincide with its (vectorial) free cumulants:

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{n}(t,\bar{t})}&\textrm{ if }{\bm{\sigma}}=(\sigma,\ldots,\sigma)\textrm{ for some }\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.\;.

(124)

Furthermore, the asymptotically normalized pure random tensor
T/\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})
has the same distribution as the complex Gaussian
T_{\mathbbm{1}}
:

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})\bigr)^{n}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\overline{t_{1}})\;,

(125)

so that the scaling in (
121
) is sharp if
\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})\neq 0
.

Proof.

Starting from (
113
), one sees from (
115
) that the term corresponding to
\Pi
in the rightmost sum scales as
N^{D\#(\Pi)-nD+n(1-D)}
, so that the term that dominates the sum over
\Pi\geq\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta)
is
\Pi=\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta)
, with
\#(\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta))=n
. This imposes the condition
\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1_{n,\bar{n}}
:

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1}\end{subarray}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})+n(1-D)}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta),\eta}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr)\;.

We may write the exponent of
N
as
n-d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)
, which implies (
122
). For any
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
,
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta),\eta}(t,\bar{t})=\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\mathrm{id}_{1}}(t,\bar{t})^{n}=\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})^{n}
, so that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{\delta({\bm{\sigma}})-n}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t,\bar{t})^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1}\end{subarray}}1\;,

where we recognize
\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\overline{t_{1}})
, (
44
). This shows (
125
). The relations (
123
) and (
124
) follow from (
98
).
∎

For the standard complex Gaussian, we identify from (
112
) and (
113
) for
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\sigma}[T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T_{\mathbbm{1}}}]=N^{1-D}\delta_{n,1}\;,

(126)

for which the rightmost sum in (
112
) is one (for
\Pi=\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta)
), and the rightmost sum in (
113
) is
\delta_{K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta),1}
, so that we indeed recover (
37
). Since
(N^{D-1}T_{\mathbbm{1}},N^{D-1}\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
scales as (
114
), we find:

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\bar{t}_{1})=\delta_{n,1}\;,

(127)

in accordance with (6.3) of
[
CGL25
]
.

4
Tensorial free cumulants and the matrix product scaling

In this section, we study mixed tensors whose classical cumulants have the same scaling in
N
as tensor products of independent random matrices, called “matrix product scaling”. This scaling was considered by Nechita and Park in
[
NP25
]
. In this paper, the authors introduce a notion of tensorial free cumulants for all connected
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and study their properties. As far as we understand, this notion is postulated, and not obtained as a limit of finite size quantities. Here, we will show that these relations are obtained as limits of the finite size relations of Sec.
2.8.2
for connected invariants, and extend them to asymptotic cumulants for arbitrary non-connected
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, in analogy to higher order free cumulants
[
Col+07
]
.

The same scaling was considered in
[
CGL23a
]
for random tensor taking uniformly at random in LU orbits, for which the tensorial free cumulants
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\rm m}
coincide with the
\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}
derived from the tensor HCIZ integral, and computed for any tensor in the orbit. In that case, the same formulae as in
[
NP25
]
had been obtained in
[
CGL23a
]
asymptotically for first order (melonic) invariants. In the general case of a mixed random tensor satisfying the matrix product scaling ansatz, the aforementioned fact implies a generalization of the relation that links the asymptotics of the free energy of the HCIZ integral and the primitive of the R-transform for random matrices. We detail this below.

4.1
The matrix product scaling

We consider a mixed LU-invariant random tensor
A
scaling as:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=N^{2(1-K({\bm{\sigma}}))+\#{\bm{\sigma}}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr),

(128)

where we recall that
\#\bm{\sigma}=\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#\sigma_{c}
. For
D=1
, we indeed recover (
105
).

If
A=A^{\prime}
or
A=UA^{\prime}U^{\dagger}
with
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D}
,
U_{c}
Haar distributed, one has (
32
):

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a^{\prime})=\delta_{1_{n},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-\#{\bm{\sigma}}}\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A^{\prime})\;,

(129)

and more generally for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-\#{\bm{\sigma}}}\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A^{\prime})=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a^{\prime})\;.

(130)

Lemma 4.1
.

Consider a LU-invariant mixed random tensor
A
satisfying the scaling assumption (
128
). Then the moments of
A
factorize asymptotically. Indeed, for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{\#{\bm{\sigma}}}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A)\Bigr]=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)\;,

where if
{\bm{\sigma}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\sigma}}_{K({\bm{\sigma}})}
are the connected components of
{\bm{\sigma}}
, then
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)=\prod_{i=1}^{K({\bm{\sigma}})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{i}}(a)
.

Proof.

The term corresponding to
\pi
in the classical moment-cumulant formula (
29
) scales as
N
to the power
2(\#(\pi)-K({\bm{\sigma}}))+\#\bm{\sigma}
. The dominant term is therefore obtained for
\pi=\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})
. ∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Consider
A_{1},\ldots,A_{D}
some
N\times N
unitarily invariant random matrices behaving asymptotically as (
105
). Then
A=\bigotimes_{c}A_{c}
satisfies (
128
). If the
A_{c}
are all independent and
{\bm{\sigma}}
is connected, then

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}),\sigma_{c}}(a_{c})=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{\eta_{c}\in\sigma_{c}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\eta_{c}}(a_{c})\;,

where
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\eta_{c}}(a_{c})=\lim_{N}\frac{1}{N}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr(A_c^{\ell(\eta_c)})\Bigr]
, in which
\ell(\eta_{c})
is the number of elements in the cycle
\eta_{c}
.

Proof.

Consider
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with connected components
{\bm{\sigma}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{\sigma}}^{(p)}
,
p=K({\bm{\sigma}})\geq 1
. We have:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=k_{p}\biggl(\biggl\{\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{\eta_{c}^{(i)}\in\sigma_{c}^{(i)}}\Tr(A_c^{ \ell(\eta_c^{(i)}) } )\biggr\}_{1\leq i\leq p}\biggr)\;,

where the products are over the disjoint cycles
\eta_{c}^{(i)}
of
\sigma_{c}^{(i)}
.
Applying the formula of Leonov and Shiryaev
[
LS59
]
:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\in\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})}\\
{\pi\vee\pi_{0}=1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{B\in\pi}k_{\lvert B\rvert}\biggl(\biggl\{\Tr(A_c^{ \ell(\eta_c^{(i)}) } )\biggr\}_{\eta_{c}^{(i)}\in B_{c}}\biggr)\;,

where
\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})
is the set of partitions of the
\sum_{c,i}\#(\sigma_{c}^{(i)})=\#\bm{\sigma}
disjoint cycles of the
\sigma_{c}^{(i)}
,
\pi_{0}\in\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})
has
p
blocks, each gathering the cycles of the
\sigma_{c}^{(i)}
for
i
fixed and for all
1\leq c\leq D
, and
1
is the one block partition in
\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})
, and if
\pi\in\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})
and
{}^{B}\in\pi
,
B_{c}
is
B
restricted to the cycles of
\sigma_{c}^{(i)}
. Since the
A_{c}
satisfy (
105
), each term in the sum scales as
N
to the power
2\#(\pi)-\#\bm{\sigma}
. Under the constraint
\pi\vee\pi_{0}=1
, one has applying (
13
):

L\bigl[\pi,\pi_{0};0_{\bm{\sigma}}\bigr]=\#\bm{\sigma}-p-\#(\pi)+1\geq 0\;,

(131)

where
0_{\bm{\sigma}}
is the trivial partition in
\mathcal{P}({\bm{\sigma}})
, and the inequality is saturated for some
\pi
. Therefore,
\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]
scales as
2(1-p+\#\bm{\sigma})-\#\bm{\sigma}=2(1-K({\bm{\sigma}}))+\#\bm{\sigma}
.

If the
A_{c}
are independent and
{\bm{\sigma}}
is connected,
p=1
so that
\#\bm{\sigma}-\#(\pi)\geq 0
with equality if and only if
\pi
is the trivial partition
0_{\bm{\sigma}}
:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\Bigl(\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{\eta_{c}\in\sigma_{c}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr A_{c}^{\ell(\eta_{c}^{(i)})}\Bigr]+o(1)\Bigr)\;,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Order of dominance.

As explained in Sec.
2.5
, we call first order or dominant the
{\bm{\sigma}}
for which the matrix scaling is maximal (the exponent of
N
in (
128
)), up to a rescaling of the tensors
A
. According to Thm.
2.6
, one has:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=N^{1+n(D-1)-(K({\bm{\sigma}})-1)-\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)(1+o(1))\;,

(132)

so that the first-order
{\bm{\sigma}}
are connected and melonic and such that the canonical pairing is the identity. We let:

\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}=\bigl\{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\ \mid\ K({\bm{\sigma}})=1\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0\bigr\}\;.

(133)

The second-order
{\bm{\sigma}}
are either two connected melonic graphs with the same labeling condition, or one connected graph with
\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=1

(see
[
BLT17
,
FLT20
]
), and so on.

We say that the distribution of
A
is characterized asymptotically at order
k
by the data of the
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)
, for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
satisfying:

\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})+K({\bm{\sigma}})\leq k\;.

(134)

4.2
Tensorial free cumulants of arbitrary order

The non-negative combinatorial quantities
L
and
L_{D}
have been respectively defined in (
13
) and (
14
). One has in particular
7
7

7

In
[
CGL23a
]
, this quantity has been introduced as
\Box_{\bm{\sigma}}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
.
for
{\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

\displaystyle L\bigl[\Pi({\bm{\tau}},{\bm{\sigma}}),\pi;\Pi({\bm{\tau}})\bigr]

\displaystyle=K({\bm{\tau}})-K({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})-\#(\pi)+\#(\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}))\;,

(135)

\displaystyle L_{D}\Bigl[\bigl\{\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\bigr\},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}});\bigl\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{c=1}^{D}\bigl(\#(\sigma_{c})-\#(\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c}))\bigr)-K({\bm{\sigma}})+K({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})\;.

(136)

The genus
g
has been introduced in (
11
). We define the following sets:

\mathbb{G}({\bm{\sigma}})=\Bigl\{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\quad\mid\quad\forall c,\ g(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad L_{D}\bigl[\bigl\{\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\bigr\},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}\bigr]=0\Bigr\}\;,

(137)

which is not empty since for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{G}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}})
. We also define:
8
8

8

Note that
\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
actually only depends on
\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})
and
\Pi({\bm{\tau}})
.

\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=\Bigl\{\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)\ \mid\ \pi\geq\Pi({\bm{\tau}})\quad\textrm{and}\quad L\bigl[\Pi({\bm{\tau}},{\bm{\sigma}}),\pi;\Pi({\bm{\tau}})\bigr]=0\Bigr\}\;.

(138)

It is also non-empty, since for any
{\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
\Pi({\bm{\tau}})\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
.
These two sets allows us to define the notion of
forest of permutations
. At this point the definition is simple. It will be more involved in the pure case (see Definition
5.4
).

Definition 4.3

(Mixed forest of permutations)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. The set of mixed forests
of permutations on
\bm{\sigma}
in the mixed case is

\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})=\Bigl\{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})\times\mathcal{P}(n)\colon\Pi\in\mathbb{P}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})\Bigr\}.

The following theorem generalizes the relations between asymptotic cumulants and higher-order free cumulants for unitarily invariant random matrices (
D=1
). More precisely, it generalizes the formulation of these relations given in
[
Lio22
, Theorem 3.1]
. The combinatorial quantity
\Gamma
has been defined in (
55
).

Theorem 4.4
.

For
A
mixed with
D\geq 1
inputs, LU-invariant, and that scales like (
128
), we have:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]=N^{2(1-K({\bm{\sigma}}))+\#{\bm{\sigma}}-nD}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\;,

(139)

where the tensorial free cumulants of
A
of arbitrary order are given
by the relations:

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\ \Gamma\bigl[\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]\;,

(140)

where the sums are non-empty. The inverse relations are given by:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}({\bm{\sigma}})}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\\
{\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\;,

(141)

where the sums are non-empty. For connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
, these relations read:

\displaystyle\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\leq{\bm{\sigma}}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\;,

(142)

\displaystyle\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\leq{\bm{\sigma}}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\mathsf{M}({\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1})\;.

(143)

If
A^{\prime}
is deterministic and if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is connected, these relations read:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\leq{\bm{\sigma}}}\lambda^{\rm m}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a^{\prime})\;,\qquad\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\leq{\bm{\sigma}}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a^{\prime})\mathsf{M}({\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1})\;,

(144)

where
\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})
is the rescaled limit of
\mathcal{L}^{\rm m}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A^{\prime}]
defined in (
62
).

If
A^{\prime}=\mathbbm{1}
, the
N^{D}\times N^{D}
identity matrix, one has from (
65
) for
{\bm{\sigma}}
connected:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(1)=1\;,\hskip 28.45274pt\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(1)=\delta_{n,1}\;,

(145)

where we use the notation
a^{\prime}=1
.

If
A=UA^{\prime}U^{\dagger}
with
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D}
,
U_{c}
Haar distributed, then
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})
.

The proof of the theorem relies on the following lemma:

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)
, and

\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi
. We
have

2(\#\pi-K(\bm{\rho}))+\#\bm{\rho}\leq 2(\#\pi\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})-K(\bm{\sigma}))+d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+\#\bm{\sigma},

with equality if and only if
(\bm{\rho},\pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
,
i.e.
\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})
and

\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})
.

Proof.

We start by noticing that by Euler’s formula (
11
),

\begin{split}\#\bm{\rho}-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})&=\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(\#\rho_{c}+\#\sigma_{c}-n+\#(\rho_{c}\sigma_{c}^{-1})\Bigr)-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#\sigma_{c}\\
&=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(K(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})-g(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})\Bigr)-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#\sigma_{c}.\end{split}

Then, we use the notation
L_{D}
introduced in (
14
) and write

\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#\sigma_{c}-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\Pi(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})\},\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}\Bigr)-2K(\bm{\sigma})+2K(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}K(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#\sigma_{c}.

Finally, we use the notation
L
(see (
13
)) and get

-2L\Bigl(\Pi(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}),\pi;\Pi(\bm{\rho})\Bigr)+2\#\Bigl(\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi\Bigr)=2\Bigl(\#\pi-K(\bm{\rho})\Bigr)+2K(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}).

Putting everything together, we end up with

\begin{split}2\Bigl(\#\pi-&K(\bm{\rho})\Bigr)+\#\bm{\rho}=2\Bigl(\#(\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi)-K(\bm{\sigma})\Bigr)+d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})+\#\bm{\sigma}\\
&-2\sum_{c=1}^{D}g(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})-2L\Bigl(\Pi(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}),\pi;\Pi(\bm{\rho})\Bigr)-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\Pi(\rho_{c},\sigma_{c})\},\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}\Bigr).\end{split}

(146)

We observe that the quantity appearing in the second line of
(
146
) are non-positive. They are zero if and only if

\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})
and
\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

We start from the expression (
71
) of the finite
N

precursors
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]
. The tensor
A
is assumed
to scale as in (
128
), and the asymptotics of

{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}
are given in Theorem

2.14
, leading to:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\pi\geq\Pi({\bm{\tau}})}N^{2(\#(\pi)-K({\bm{\tau}}))+\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\tau_{c})+\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\tau_{c}^{-1})-2(\#(\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}))-1)-2nD}\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\ \Gamma\bigl[\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]+o(1)\Bigr)\;.\end{split}

(147)

We rewrite the exponent of
N
using Lemma
4.5
. We get

\begin{split}2(\#(\pi)-K({\bm{\tau}}))+\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\tau_{c})&+\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\tau_{c}^{-1})-2(\#(\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}))-1)-2nD\\
&=2(\#(\pi)-K({\bm{\tau}}))-nD+\#\bm{\tau}-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})-2(\#(\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}))-1)\\
&\leq 2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))-nD+\#\bm{\sigma},\end{split}

with equality if and only if
(\bm{\tau},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
. This yields the first claim

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]=N^{2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}-nD)}\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\ \Gamma\bigl[\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]+o(1)\;.

Since for any
{\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
the sets
\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})
and
\mathbb{P}_{{\rm m}}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
are
non-empty, this gives
(
139
) and
(
140
).

On the other hand, for the
\Phi^{{\rm m}}
one has from
(
74
) and
(
139
):

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\geq\Pi({\bm{\tau}})}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\pi=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\ N^{2(\#(\pi)-K({\bm{\tau}}))+\sum_{c}\#(\tau_{c})+\sum_{c}\#(\sigma_{c}\tau_{c}^{-1})-nD}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\ \;.

(148)

Subtracting
nD
to the exponent of
N
, one recover the exponent in
(
147
), under the constraint

\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\pi=1_{n}
. It remains to show that there exists at
least one
{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}({\bm{\sigma}})
and

\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{{\rm m}}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
such that
\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})=1
. One
can verify that
{\bm{\tau}}={\bm{\sigma}}
does not work in general. Given

{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, we choose
\tau_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
that connects

{\bm{\sigma}}
in a minimal way: for every connected component

B\in\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})
, we choose one cycle
\eta_{B}\in\sigma_{1}
of

\sigma_{1}
whose support is in
B
and let
\eta
be the disjoint
union of the
\eta_{B}
. One has
\#(\eta)=K({\bm{\sigma}})
We choose a cyclic
permutation
\gamma
on the support of
\eta
, such that
\eta
forms
a non-crossing permutation on
\gamma
:

\#(\eta)+\#(\eta\gamma^{-1})=1+\ell
, where

\ell=\sum_{B\in\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})}\ell(\eta_{B})
is the total number of
elements in the cycles
\{\eta_{B}\}
. We then define
\tau_{1}
as the
disjoint union of
\eta
and of the cycles of
\sigma_{1}
that differ
from the
\eta_{B}
. One has
K({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=1
, and

K(\sigma_{1},\tau_{1})=\#(\tau_{1})=\#(\sigma_{1})-K({\bm{\sigma}})+1
, and

\sigma_{1}\preceq\tau_{1}
:

\#(\sigma_{1})+\#(\sigma_{1}\tau_{1}^{-1})=\#(\tau_{1})+n
. In
particular,
g(\sigma_{1},\tau_{1})=0
.
Furthermore,
L_{D}[\{\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}]=\#(\sigma_{1})-K(\sigma_{1},\tau_{1})-K({\bm{\sigma}})+1=0
, so that
{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}})
. With this choice,
\Pi(\tau)\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})
. The dominant scaling in
N
is therefore coherent with (
128
), and the rescaled limit is (
141
).
∎

Remark 4.6
.

In Theorem
4.4
, it is shown that the dominant scale in
N
of the finite size precursors
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]
is the same as that of
\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]
to a factor
N^{-nD}
. This shows in particular that

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]=N^{1-n-(K({\bm{\sigma}})-1)-\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

(149)

In (
87
), the joint cumulants of the tensor components are shown to expand on the
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[A]
, so that the order of contribution of
{\bm{\sigma}}
as defined above can also be interpreted as the order of contribution in
N
of
{\bm{\sigma}}
to the microscopic description of the distribution (
87
).

Lemma 4.7

(see also Lemma 4.4 of
[
NP25
]
)
.

Consider
A_{1},\ldots,A_{D}
some
N\times N
unitarily invariant random matrices behaving asymptotically as (
105
). Then the tensorial free cumulants of
A=\bigotimes_{c}A_{c}
for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
purely connected are given by:

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}),\sigma_{c}}(a_{c}).

Proof.

Combining (
143
) with Lemma
4.2
, one has:

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\sum_{\tau_{c}\preceq\sigma_{c}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\tau_{c}),\tau_{c}}(a)\mathsf{M}(\sigma_{c}\tau_{c}^{-1}),

where for each
c
we recognize
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}),\sigma_{c}}(a_{c})
.
∎

4.3
Earlier results on tensorial free cumulants and the tensor HCIZ integral

Purely connected tensorial free cumulants.

As mentioned at the beginning of this section, in
[
NP25
]
, the authors define tensorial free cumulants for connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
through the relations (
142
) and (
143
). As far as we understand, these relations are stated in this form, and it is shown using these relations that independent LU-invariant mixed random tensors satisfying the matrix product scaling are tensorially free, in the sense that free cumulants involving these different tensors vanish (see Thm. 7.7 of this reference for the precise statement). This implies the additivity of tensorial free cumulants for independent LU-invariant random tensors of this kind.

In Thm.
4.4
, we obtain the same relations as limits of the finite size relations, and the additivity of tensorial free cumulants for independent LU-invariant random tensors satisfying the matrix product ansatz is a consequence of Prop.
2.18
.

Melonic free cumulants and the tensor HCIZ integral.

The relation on the right-hand side of (
76
) was obtained in
[
CGL23a
]
for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}
.
9
9

9

This is because
[
CGL23a
]
focused on computing the large
N
asymptotics of the cumulants of the HCIZ integral, which expand over the first order invariants only.
We formulate this in the following theorem, which generalizes the relation between the free-energy of the HCIZ integral and the primitive of the R-transform, given in the matrix case in
[
Col03
]
(see also
[
Col+07
, Theorem 8.5]
,
[
GZ02
,
GM05
]
).

Theorem 4.8

(
[
CGL23a
]
)
.

Consider
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and a deterministic tensor
B
with
D\geq 1
inputs such that for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(B)=t_{\bm{\sigma}}<\infty
.
If
A
is a mixed LU-invariant random tensor with
D
inputs and that scales as (
128
):

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[dU]\ \mathrm{e}^{zN\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A U^\dagger)}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=n!\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{\rm m}}\frac{t_{\bm{\sigma}}}{\#\Aut_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\;\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)\;,

where the sum is over elements in the quotient
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{\rm m}
that are first order, i.e. connected and such that
\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0
.
If
B
has only one non-vanishing element
B_{1,\ldots,1;1,\ldots,1}=1
, then for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
t_{\bm{\sigma}}=1
.

Proof.

If
A^{\prime}
is a deterministic tensor scaling as
(
129
), the term that dominates
(
64
) when
N
goes to infinity has been
computed in Lem. 5.3 of
[
CGL23a
]
, where it is
shown that:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\log\int[dU]\ \mathrm{e}^{zN\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A' U^\dagger)}\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}}t_{\bm{\sigma}}\;\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})\;.

(150)

Furthermore, using the automorphism groups introduced in (
24
), we have for any
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\#\Bigl\{\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}\colon\bm{\sigma}\sim_{{\rm m}}\bm{\sigma^{\prime}}\Bigr\}=\frac{n!}{\#\Aut_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})},

and the number
\#\Aut_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
does not depend on the
representative in the equivalence class by
\sim_{{\rm m}}
. Hence, we
get the result in the non-random case.

For the random case, the statement follows from
(
75
) which states that for

{\bm{\sigma}}
connected (which is true for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}
),

\mathds{E}[\mathcal{L}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]]=\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]
, so that their
rescaled limits match.

Finally, if
B
has only one non-zero coefficient

B_{1,\ldots,1;1,\ldots,1}=1
, then we immediately have that

\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(B)=1
.
∎

This statement formally implies that (as a formal series):

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathds{E}\biggl[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\log\int[dU]\ \mathrm{e}^{zN\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A U^\dagger)}\biggr]=\sum_{n\geq 1}z^{n}\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{\rm m}}\frac{t_{\bm{\sigma}}}{\#\Aut_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\;\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)\;,

(151)

and similarly for the deterministic case. For
D=1
, the first order
\sigma
are cyclic permutations of
n
elements, and for any such permutation one obtains the same result
\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(a)=\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{n}(a)
, which is the free cumulant of
A
in the usual sense (see e.g.
[
NS06
]
). Since there are
(n-1)!
such permutations, Thm.
4.8
simplifies for
B
with only one one non-vanishing element
B_{1;1}=1
, to:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int dU\ \mathrm{e}^{zN\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A U^\dagger)}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=(n-1)!\;\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{n}(a)\;,

(152)

or as formal series:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathds{E}\biggl[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\log\int dU\ \mathrm{e}^{zN\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A U^\dagger)}\biggr]=R_{A}(z)\;,

(153)

where
R_{A}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}z^{n-1}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{n}(a)
is the R-transform of
A
, see for instance
[
MS17
]
.

4.4
Relation to the tensor HCIZ integral in the disconnected case

If
A=A^{\prime}
or
A=UA^{\prime}U^{\dagger}
with
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D}
,
U_{c}
Haar distributed, one has:

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\ \varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a^{\prime})\ \Gamma\bigl[\Pi({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]\;.

(154)

The inverse relations are given by:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a^{\prime})=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}})}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm m}({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\\
{\pi\vee\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\lambda^{\rm m}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a^{\prime})\;.

(155)

If
A^{\prime}=\mathbbm{1}
, the
N^{D}\times N^{D}
identity matrix, one has from (
65
) for any
{\bm{\sigma}}
:

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(1)=\delta_{1_{n},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}})}\;,\hskip 28.45274pt\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(1)=\delta_{n,1}\;.

(156)

For
{\bm{\sigma}}
satisfying
\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0
, the expression (
154
) is obtained from the tensor HCIZ integral. The following generalizes Thm.
4.8
in the case where
A
is deterministic or uniform in a LU orbit.

Theorem 4.9
.

Consider two deterministic tensors
A,B
with
D\geq 1
inputs each, that scale as (
129
), and
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
. Then:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\log\int[dU]\ \mathrm{e}^{zN^{2-D}\Tr(B^\mathsf{T} U A U^\dagger)}\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D},}\\
{\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(b)\;\lambda^{\rm m}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)\;,

where the sum is over elements
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
that are satisfy
\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0
but are not necessarily connected.

Proof.

Following the notations of
[
CGL23a
]
, we introduce the following combinatorial quantity:

\Delta({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=K({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})+n(D-1)-\sum_{c=1}^{D}K(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\;.

(157)

As stated in Prop. 4.8 of
[
CGL23a
]
, one has that:

\Delta({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=L_{D}\bigl[\bigl\{\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\bigr\},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}\bigr]+\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})\;.

Since both terms on the right-hand side are non-negative:

Lemma 4.10
.

A pair
({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\times\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
satisfies
\Delta({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=0
if and only if it satisfies:

\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad L_{D}\bigl[\bigl\{\Pi(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})\bigr\},\Pi({\bm{\sigma}});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c})\}\bigr]=0\;,

if and only if these conditions are satisfied with the roles of
{\bm{\sigma}}
and
{\bm{\tau}}
exhanged.

We define the set:

\mathbb{G}^{0,n}=\Bigl\{({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\times\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\quad\mid\quad\forall c,\ g(\sigma_{c},\tau_{c})=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\Delta({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})=0\Bigr\}\;.

(158)

If
A,B
scale as (
129
), it was shown in Thm. 7 of of
[
CGL23a
]
(regime S-II) that for
D\geq 1
:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{n}}\log\mathcal{I}_{D,N}\bigl[A;zN^{2-D}B\bigr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=\sum_{({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})\in\mathbb{G}^{0,n}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(b)\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\Gamma\bigl[\Pi({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]\;.

(159)

Thanks to Lem.
4.10
, we can split the sums over
{\bm{\sigma}}
and
{\bm{\tau}}
as:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{n}}\log\mathcal{I}_{D,N}\bigl[A;zN^{2-D}B\bigr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\\
{\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(b)\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{G}({\bm{\sigma}})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\Gamma\bigl[\Pi({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]\;,

where we recognize
\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)
(
155
). ∎

On the right-hand side of the last formula from the proof,
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)
is the rescaled limit of the trace-invariant
\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(A)
, see (
130
). If now
A
is random, taking the expectation of this quantity, one has on the right-hand side the rescaled limit:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-\#\bm{\tau}}\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{{\bm{\tau}}}(A)]=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\tau}}),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\;,

thanks to the asymptotic factorization shown in Lem.
4.1
. The right-hand side then coincides with
\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}},\;\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)
if the
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,{\bm{\tau}}}(a)
vanish for
\pi>\Pi({\bm{\tau}})
, as in (
129
).
Said otherwise, in that case, one can exchange the expectation and the logarithm.

5
Tensorial free cumulants and Gaussian scalings

Starting from the example of the Gaussian tensor discussed in Section

2.6
, we introduce two scaling assumptions for
the classical cumulants of pure random tensors. The two scaling
exponents,
\delta
and
\varepsilon
, appear in a crucial way when computing the
free cumulants of pure tensors.

5.1
Two scalings for pure tensors

With the result for Gaussian tensors in mind (see Section

2.6
), a natural scaling assumption to make is
to consider random LU-invariant pure tensors
T,\bar{T}
whose
classical cumulants satisfy

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n-\delta(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr).

(160)

In Section
5.2
, we give the leading
asymptotics of the finite-
N
precursors to the free cumulants at
all-order. The case of the finite-
N
precursors to the free cumulant
at first order was studied in details in
[
CGL25
]
.
More precisely, Collins, Gurau, and Lionni gave formulae to compute
the asymptotics of
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]
when

K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1
. We extend these results to the case of
any value of
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]
. The leading order of

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]
is then shown to be

N^{n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}
, where the exponent

\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})
is defined for any
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
by

\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})=\min_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\Bigl(2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr),

(161)

and its multiplicative extensions: for any
\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)

\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})=\min_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\leq\Pi}\Bigl(2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr),

(162)

The corresponding sets of minimizers are

\begin{split}\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})&=\Bigl\{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\colon\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})=2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr\},\\
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})&=\bigl\{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\colon\Pi(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\leq\Pi,\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})=2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\bigr\}.\end{split}

(163)

If
\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}
, we write
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(1_{n,\bar{n}},\bm{\sigma})
.

We recall the definition given in
(
45
) for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
and

\Pi\geq\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
:

\delta(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)=\Pi\end{subarray}}d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)

The two scaling exponents
\delta
and
\varepsilon
(recall
(
160
) and (
165
)) of classical
cumulants agree if and only

\delta(\bm{\sigma})=\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})-2
. This equality is
related to the notion of decreasing tuple in Lemma

5.2
.

Definition 5.1

(Decreasing tuple)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. The
D
-tuple of
permutations
\bm{\sigma}
is said to be
decreasing
if for all

\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
satisfying
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi
,

2+\delta(\bm{\sigma})\leq 2\#\Pi+\delta(\Pi,\bm{\sigma}).

Given
\tilde{\Pi}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
with

\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\tilde{\Pi}
, we say that
\bm{\sigma}
is
decreasing
from

\tilde{\Pi}
if for every
\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n),\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\leq\tilde{\Pi}
, we
have

2\#\tilde{\Pi}+\delta(\tilde{\Pi},\bm{\sigma})\leq 2\#\Pi+\delta(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
, and

{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. We have

\delta(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})\geq\epsilon(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})-2\#\Pi\;,

with equality if and only if
\bm{\sigma}
is decreasing from
\Pi
.

A purely connected
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
is automatically decreasing. This gives the following corollary.

Corollary 5.3
.

Consider
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. If

K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})=1
, then
\delta({\bm{\sigma}})=\epsilon({\bm{\sigma}})-2
.

Proof of the lemma.

We are going to show the Lemma in the case

\tilde{\Pi}=1_{n,\bar{n}}
. In general, the result will follow
from applying the result on the blocks of
\tilde{\Pi}
.

To show the first statement, we write:

\epsilon(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi^{\prime}\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in S_{n}}\\
{\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\Pi}\end{subarray}}\bigl[2\#\Pi^{\prime}+d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)\bigr]=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi^{\prime}\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\bigl[2\#\Pi^{\prime}+\delta(\Pi^{\prime},{\bm{\sigma}})\bigr]\leq 2\#\Pi+\delta(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})\;.

Equality holds if and only if the minimum is attained for

\Pi^{\prime}=\Pi
, which concludes the proof.
∎

5.2
Pure random tensors that scale like the standard complex Gaussian

Let us now concentrate on pure random tensors
(T,\bar{T})
that
satisfy the scaling assumption (
160
).

5.2.1
Tensorial free cumulants of arbitrary order

The expression of the free cumulants are expressed using a
combinatorial object we call pure forest of permutations, defined
similarly as in the mixed case in Definition
4.3
.

Definition 5.4

(Gaussian forest of
permutations)
.

Let

n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. A
Gaussian forest of permutations on
(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
in the Gaussian
case is a pair

(\bm{\rho},\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta))\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\times\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)

such that
\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
.
The set of Gaussian forests of permutations on
\bm{\sigma}
is then
defined by

\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})=\Bigl\{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}\times\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\colon\exists\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\cap\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho}),\Pi=\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta),\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\Bigr\}.

Remark 5.5

(
\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})
is not empty)
.

The set
\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})
is not empty in general: let

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
and take
\bm{\rho}=(\eta,\ldots,\eta)
. We immediately get that
\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}=\bm{\mathrm{id}_{n}}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
, and
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\eta),\bm{\rho})
. Hence,

\Biggl\{\Bigl((\eta,\ldots,\eta),\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta)\Bigr)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\colon\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\Biggr\}\subset\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma}).

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
(T,\bar{T})
be a pure random tensor such that for all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
we have

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n-\delta(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr).

Then, we have for all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n(1-D)+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)

where

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})\ \Gamma\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\Pi,\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}\Bigr].

Furthermore, whenever
K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1
, we have the inverse
relation

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})\\
\bm{\rho}\text{ decreasing from }\Pi\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t}).

The proof relies on the following description of the exponent

\delta(\bm{\sigma})
using the set

\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
, and

\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with

\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi
. For all

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
, we have

-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)\leq 2\Bigl(\#(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta))-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho}),

with equality if and only if

\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
.

Proof of Theorem
5.6
.

Let us start by using (
78
), which yields

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[T,\bar{T}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}].

Note that by hypothesis,

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[t,\bar{t}]+o(1)\Bigr),

and by (
54
), since
\Pi(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\leq\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\Bigr)_{[n]}=\Bigl(\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\Bigr)_{[n]}
, we get

{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}(\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})=N^{2(1-\#(\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})))-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})-nD}\Bigl(\Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}]+o(1)\Bigr).

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
. We have

\Pi=\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)
and
\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})=d(\bm{\rho},\eta)
, and the product

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[T,\bar{T}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}]

is of order

N^{n+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho},\eta)))-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})-nD}.

By Lemma
5.7
, we get

n(1-D)+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho},\eta))-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})\leq n(1-D)+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta),

with equality if and only if

(\bm{\rho},\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta))\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
. Finally,
the quantity

2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
is
maximal when
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
. Together with the Definition

5.4
of the Gaussian forests of
permutation, this implies the result.

Let us show the inverse relation. By Lemma
2.17
, we have

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}N^{nD-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\\
{\Pi_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}}\end{subarray}}\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,{\bm{\tau}}}[T,\bar{T}]\;.

By using the scaling assumption (
160
) on the
left-hand side and the first claim on the right-hand side, we have

N^{n-\delta(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi\\
{\Pi_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}}\end{subarray}}\ N^{-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n+2-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\rho})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

Lemma
5.2
then implies that the exponent of
N
satisfies

-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n+2-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\rho})\leq n-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho}),

with equality if and only if
\bm{\rho}
is decreasing from
\Pi
.
Then, for every
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
, we have by Lemma
5.7
and
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1_{n,\bar{n}}

-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n+2-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\rho})\leq n+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\leq n-\delta(\bm{\sigma}),

and equality if and only if
\bm{\rho}
is decreasing from
\Pi
,

\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
, and

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
. We start by noticing
that

\begin{split}-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})&=\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(\#(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1})+\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-nD+\#(\rho_{c}\sigma_{c}^{-1})\Bigr)-nD-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\\
&=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(K(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-g(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\Bigr)-nD-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}).\end{split}

Then, we use the notation
L_{D}
introduced in (
14
) and write

\begin{split}\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\Pi(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\},&\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\}\Bigr)-2K(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})+2K(\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1},\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\\
&=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}K(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}).\end{split}

Putting everything together, we end up with

\begin{split}-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)&=2\Bigl(\#(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta))-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})\\
&\quad\quad-2\sum_{c=1}^{D}g(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-2L\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma},\eta),\Pi;\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr).\end{split}

(164)

The last line in (
164
) is a sum of non-positive
terms. These terms are zero if and only if

\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
.
∎

5.3
Beyond the Gaussian scaling

For random matrices or for the random tensors considered in Sec.
4
, the dominant scales in
N
of the
\Phi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{m}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{m}}
differ by a factor
N^{-nD}
(see Thm.
4.4
). This, together with Theorem
5.6
, is a motivation for studying pure random tensors for which the
N^{-nD}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}
scale as
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{\sigma}}
. This is not a simple curiosity: we will show in Sec.
6
that Gaussians whose covariances are tensor products of independent random matrices from classical random matrix ensembles (Wishart with parameters of the same order, GUE…) satisfy these scaling assumptions.

In this section, we consider pure random tensors such that for all

n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{2+n-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\bigr),

(165)

where
\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})
does not depend on
N
and

\varepsilon
was defined in (
161
) by

\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})=\min_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\Bigl(2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)\;.

5.3.1
Comparison with the Gaussian scaling

Similarly to Thm.
2.6
, one has the following.

Proposition 5.8
.

For any
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, one has

2+n-\epsilon({\bm{\sigma}})\leq 1-(K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})-1)\;,

(166)

with equality if and only if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic. Furthermore, if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic, its canonical pairing is the unique element in
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
.

Proof.

Combining Thm.
2.6
with (
45
), one sees that

\delta(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})\geq n-\#\Pi+(D-1)(K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})-\#\Pi)=n+(D-1)K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})-D\#\Pi\;,

with equality if and only if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic. As a consequence,

2\#\Pi+\delta(\Pi,{\bm{\sigma}})\geq n+K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})+(D-2)(K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})-\#\Pi)\geq n+K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})\;,

with equality if and only
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic and
\Pi=\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
. Therefore,

\epsilon({\bm{\sigma}})\geq n+K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})\;,

with equality if and only if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic. Furthermore, if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is melonic,
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
contains the unique
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
such that
K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=K_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
and

d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta(\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}})
(Thm.
2.6
) .
∎

We say that
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
factorizes at large
N
for the pure random tensor
(T,\bar{T})
if

\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T,\bar{T})\bigr]\ \underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\ \prod_{i=1}^{q}\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{i}}(T,\bar{T})\bigr]

(167)

where
{\bm{\sigma}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\sigma}}_{q}
are the pure connected components of
{\bm{\sigma}}
. It has been shown recently that not all
{\bm{\sigma}}
factorize at large
N
for the standard Gaussian
[
GJS25
,
BK26
]
. We say on the other hand that
{\bm{\sigma}}
does not factorize at all at large
N
if the dominant scale in
N
of
\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T,\bar{T})\bigr]
is the same as that of
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]
. From (
29
), this is equivalent to the fact that for every pure partition
\Pi\in\mathcal{P_{\rm p}}(n)
satisfying
\Pi\geq\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
:

\delta({\bm{\sigma}})-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\sigma})\leq 0\;.

(168)

Since (
168
) implies that
\bm{\sigma}
is decreasing (recall Definition
5.1
), Lemma

5.2
gives the following.

Lemma 5.9
.

Consider
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
that does not factorize at all at large
N
. Then
\delta({\bm{\sigma}})=\epsilon({\bm{\sigma}})-2\;.

It is a priori not forbidden for some
{\bm{\sigma}}
that factorize at large
N
to satisfy
\delta({\bm{\sigma}})=\epsilon({\bm{\sigma}})-2
, but we do not know of any example.

In order to compare this scaling behavior with the Gaussian scaling, we consider some pure random tensors
(T_{\delta},\bar{T}_{\delta})
and
(T_{\epsilon},\bar{T}_{\epsilon})
that respectively scale as (
160
) and (
165
). For any purely connected
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, both
\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{\delta},\bar{T}_{\delta})\bigr]
and
\mathds{E}\bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{\epsilon},\bar{T}_{\epsilon})\bigr]
scale in the same way. But more generally, for the
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with fixed number of pure connected components
K({\bm{\sigma}})-1=q\geq 0
:

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{\delta},\bar{T_{\delta}}]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}N^{1-(D-1)q}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t_{\delta},\bar{t}_{\delta})+o(1)\bigr)&\textrm{ if }{\bm{\sigma}}\textrm{ melonic, }\\[4.30554pt]
o\bigl(N^{1-(D-1)q}\bigr)&\textrm{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

(169)

while on the other hand,

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{\epsilon}\bar{T_{\epsilon}}]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}N^{1-q}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}(t_{\epsilon},\bar{t}_{\epsilon})+o(1)\bigr)&\textrm{ if }{\bm{\sigma}}\textrm{ melonic, }\\[4.30554pt]
o\bigl(N^{1-q}\bigr)&\textrm{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.\;.

(170)

So while the moments associated to purely connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
have the same dominant magnitude in
N
for both (
160
) and (
165
), some of the fluctuations corresponding to classical cumulants of a fixed number of purely connected trace-invariants are “boosted ” for (
165
)
10
10

10

This can be shown to be the case for a much larger set of trace-invariants, but we do not include the proof here.
. This is not the case for the trace-invariants that do not factorize at all asymptotically.

5.3.2
Tensorial free cumulants for the scaling
\varepsilon

As in the mixed and Gaussian cases, the appropriate combinatorial
object will be the forests of permutations.

Definition 5.10

(Pure forests of permutations)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. A
pure forest of permutations on
(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
is a pair

(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\times\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
such that

•

\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
;

•

\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\leq\Pi\quad
and
\quad L\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta),\Pi;\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)=0
.

We denote by
\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
the set of forests of permutations
on
\bm{\sigma}
in the pure case.

The set of optimal forests of permutations on
\bm{\sigma}
is then defined to be

\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=\Bigl\{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}\times\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\colon\exists\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\cap\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho}),(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr\}.

Remark 5.11

(
\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
is not empty)
.

The set of pure forests of permutations is not empty. Given
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, we notice that for all
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
,
\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})
so that we get

\Bigl(\bm{\sigma},\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta).

The pure forests of permutations appear in the expression of the free
cumulants, as we now demonstrate.

Theorem 5.12
.

Let
(T,\bar{T})
be a pure LU-invariant random tensor that satisfies the
scaling assumption (
165
). For all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}

and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, we have

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n(1-D)+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)

where

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})\ \Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}].

Furthermore, we have the inverse relations

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\\
\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1_{n,\bar{n}}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t}).

The proof of Theorem
5.12
uses a
description of the exponent
\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})
in terms of forests
of permutations on
\bm{\sigma}
, provided by Lemma
5.13
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
, and

\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
with
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi
. We
have for all
\eta\in\hat{\Omega}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})

2\Bigl(\#\Pi-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)\leq 2\Bigl(\#(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi)-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho}),

with equality if and only if
(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
.

Proof of Theorem
5.12
.

We start by expressing the finite-
N
precursors to the free
cumulants in terms of the classical cumulants using
(
78
):

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,{\bm{\tau}}}[T,\bar{T}]\ {\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}\bigl[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}{\bm{\tau}}^{-1}\bigr]\;,

Using our scaling assumption (
165
) and the
asymptotic expression (
54
) of
{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}
we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}N^{n(1-D)+2\#\Pi-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\tau})-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})+2(1-\#\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}}))}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})\ \Gamma[\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}]+o(1)\Bigr)\;.\end{split}

(171)

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi,\bm{\tau})
. By Lemma
5.13
