, the exponent of
N
(minus
n(1-D)
) satisfies

2\Bigl(\#\Pi-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\tau},\eta)-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})+2\Bigl(1-\#\Pi\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\Bigr)\leq 2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta),

with equality if and only if
(\bm{\tau},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
.
The exponent of
N
takes its maximal value

n(1-D)+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})
when
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
. This
gives the first claim.

To prove the second claim, we use
(
81
):

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\\
{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}}\end{subarray}}\ N^{nD-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,{\bm{\tau}}}[T,\bar{T}]\;.

The scaling assumption (
165
) and the first claim give

N^{n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)=\sum_{{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\\
{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}})\vee\Pi=1_{n,\bar{n}}}\end{subarray}}\ N^{n+2\#\Pi-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\tau})-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)\;.

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi,\bm{\tau})
. Using Lemma
5.13
,
the exponent of
N
satisfies

n+2\#\Pi-\varepsilon(\Pi,\bm{\tau})-d({\bm{\sigma}},{\bm{\tau}})\leq n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma}),

with equality if and only if
(\Pi,\bm{\tau})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
and

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
. This gives the second claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.13
.

Let
\eta\in\hat{\Omega}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
. We start by noticing that

\begin{split}-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)-d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})&=\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(\#(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1})+\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-nD+\#(\rho_{c}\sigma_{c}^{-1})\Bigr)-nD-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\\
&=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(K(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-g(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\Bigr)-nD-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}).\end{split}

Then, we use the notation
L_{D}
introduced in (
14
) and write

\begin{split}\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\Pi(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\},&\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\}\Bigr)-2K(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})+2K(\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1},\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\\
&=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}K(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}).\end{split}

Finally, we use the notation
L
(see (
13
)) and get

-2L\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta),\Pi;\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)+2\#\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi\Bigr)=2\Bigl(\#\Pi-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)+2K(\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1},\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1}).

Putting everything together, we end up with

\begin{split}2\Bigl(\#\Pi-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)&=2\Bigl(\#(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\vee\Pi)-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})\\
&\quad\quad-2\sum_{c=1}^{D}g(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-2L\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma},\eta),\Pi;\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)\Bigr)\\
&\quad\quad-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\Pi(\rho_{c}\eta^{-1},\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\},\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1});\{\Pi(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})\}\Bigr).\end{split}

(172)

The last two lines in (
172
) are a sum of
non-positive terms. These terms are zero if and only if

(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
.
∎

5.4
Melonic free cumulants and the tensor BGW integral

Theorem 5.14
.

Consider
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and a deterministic pure tensor
J
with
D\geq 1
inputs such that for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(J,\bar{J})=t_{\bm{\sigma}}<\infty
.
If
(T,\bar{T})
is a pure LU-invariant random tensor with
D
inputs and that scales as the pure standard complex Gaussian on
D
inputs, i.e. satisfies (
160
), or that scales as (
165
):

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{n}}\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[dU]\ \mathrm{e}^{N^{D/2}(zJU\cdot T+\bar{z}\bar{J}U^{\dagger}\cdot\bar{T})}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=\bar{z}=0}=(n!)^{2}\sum_{{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}/\sim_{{\rm p}}}\frac{t_{\bm{\sigma}}}{\#\Aut_{{\rm p}}(\bm{{\bm{\sigma}}})}\;\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t,\bar{t})\;,

(173)

where the sum is over elements
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
that are first order,
i.e. purely connected and melonic (which is equivalent to connected
and such that
\Omega({\bm{\sigma}})=0
). If
J
has only one non-vanishing
element
J_{1,\ldots,1}=1
, then for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,

t_{\bm{\sigma}}=1
.

Proof.

We start by differentiating the logarithm of the BGW integral
(
59
):

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{n}}\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[\differential U]\ \mathrm{e}^{N^{D/2}(zJ\cdot T+\bar{z}\bar{J}\cdot\bar{T})}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}=N^{nD}\sum_{\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)}\mu_{\Pi}\prod_{S\in\Pi}\int[\differential U](J\cdot T)^{\#S}(\bar{J}\cdot\bar{T})^{\#S}.

Using the computation of the BGW moments (
61
), we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{n}}\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[\differential U]\ \mathrm{e}^{N^{D/2}(zJU\cdot T+\bar{z}\bar{J}U^{\dagger}\cdot\bar{T})}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}&=N^{nD}\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(J,\bar{J})\ \mathds{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{G}_{\Pi,\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]\Bigr],\\
&=N^{nD}\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(J,\bar{J})\ \mathds{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]\Bigr].\end{split}

Using the right-hand side of (
66
), we get

\begin{split}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]\Bigr]&=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(T,\bar{T})\Bigr]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}),\bm{\tau}\bm{\rho}^{-1}]\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[T,\bar{T}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}),\bm{\tau}\bm{\rho}^{-1}].\end{split}

If
(T,\bar{T})
scales as a Gaussian, i.e. satisfies (
160
), we get

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}N^{n-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})-d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho})+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho}))-nD}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})\Gamma[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}),\bm{\tau}\bm{\rho}^{-1}]+o(1)\Bigr).

Hence, we get to use Lemma
5.7
, and get that for all
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi,\bm{\rho})
the
exponent of
N
is

n-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})-d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho})+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho}))-nD\leq n+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho})+K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau},\eta)-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\eta))-d(\bm{\tau},\eta)-nD,

with equality if and only if
\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\tau}\eta^{-1})
. We then get that

n-\delta(\Pi,\bm{\rho})-d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho})+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\bm{\rho}))-nD\leq n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\tau})-nD,

with equality if and only if

\bm{\rho}\eta^{-1}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\tau}\eta^{-1})
,

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})
, and
\Pi\preceq\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau})
.
Finally, by Prop.
5.8
, we have

n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\tau})\leq 1,

with equality if and only if
\bm{\tau}
is purely connected and
melonic. We notice that if
\bm{\tau}
is purely connected, the
condition
\Pi\preceq\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau})
is
satisfied. Putting everything together and using our assumption on
(J,\bar{J})
, we get that

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{n}}&\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[\differential U]\ \mathrm{e}^{N^{D/2}(zJU\cdot T+\bar{z}\bar{J}U^{\dagger}\cdot\bar{T})}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}\\
&=N\Biggl(\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\tau})}t_{\bm{\tau}}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})\ \mathsf{M}(\bm{\tau}\bm{\rho}^{-1})+o(1)\Biggr)=N\Biggl(\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}}t_{\bm{\tau}}\ \kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Biggr).\end{split}

where
\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}
is the set of purely connected melonic

D
-tuples of permutations. We then get the result by introducing
the group of automorphism
\Aut_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
, see
(
24
).

In the case where
(T,\bar{T})
satisfies the second pure scaling
assumption (
165
), we use Lemma

5.13
and proceed as before. We obtain the same result as for the Gaussian scaling:

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{n}}&\mathds{E}\biggl[\log\int[\differential U]\ \mathrm{e}^{N^{D/2}(zJU\cdot T+\bar{z}\bar{J}U^{\dagger}\cdot\bar{T})}\biggr]\Biggr\lvert_{z=0}\\
&=N\Biggl(\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})}t_{\bm{\tau}}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})\ \mathsf{M}(\bm{\tau}\bm{\rho}^{-1})+o(1)\Biggr)=N\Biggl(\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}^{D}}t_{\bm{\tau}}\ \kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Biggr).\end{split}

The proof is concluded similarly by introducing the
1/\#\Aut_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})
factor.
∎

6
Gaussians with non-trivial covariances, random or not

6.1
The random matrix case: Ginibre and Wishart ensembles

We discuss the definition of the Wishart ensemble and the Ginibre
Ensemble with a possibly random covariance matrix.

Let
N,M\geq 1
be two integers. Let
C
be a random positive
definite matrix with law
\nu
. We denote the set of positive-definite
matrices of size
M\times M
by
\mathcal{P}(M)
. We consider the
probability distribution
\mu_{N,M}
on
{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})

defined by

\differential\mu_{N,M}(X)=\frac{1}{Z_{N,M,\nu}}\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\exp\Bigl(-N\Tr XC^{-1}X^{\dagger}\Bigr)\differential\nu(C)\differential X,

(174)

where
Z_{N,M,\nu}
is the partition function

Z_{N,M,\nu}=\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\exp\Bigl(-N\Tr XC^{-1}X^{\dagger}\Bigr)\differential\nu(C)\differential X.

Interpreting the trace
\Tr XC^{-1}X^{\dagger}
as the Hilbert-Schmidt scalar product defined by

\langle A,B\rangle_{{\rm HS}}\coloneq\Tr AB^{\dagger}\quad\text{ for }A,B\in{\rm M}_{M\times M}(\mathbb{C}),

we have
\Tr XC^{-1}X^{\dagger}=\langle C^{-1},X^{\dagger}X\rangle_{{\rm HS}}
. Hence, we
may see the integral appearing in the definition of
\mu
as the Laplace
transform of
\nu
evaluated in
NX^{\dagger}X
. It motivates us to
make the following assumption.

Hypothesis 6.1
.

The Laplace transform

\Lambda_{C}(A)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\mathrm{e}^{-\langle C^{-1},A\rangle_{{\rm HS}}}\differential\nu(C)

exists for all positive matrix
A
and decays faster than any polynomial in the entries of
A
as
\max_{i,j}|A_{ij}|\to\infty
.

Hypothesis
6.1
ensures that the measure
\mu_{N,M}

given in (
174
) is well-defined and that all its
moments are finite.

Example 6.2

(Deterministic matrix
C
)
.

If we choose
\nu=\delta_{BB^{\dagger}}
where
B
is any full-rank
matrix, then we recover the Wishart distribution with a fixed
covariance matrix. In particular, the choice
B=\Id_{M}
gives the
classical Wishart ensemble.

A different point of view is provided by performing the change of
variable
X^{\prime}=X\sqrt{C^{-1}}
. This gives the following result.

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume that
\nu
satisfies Hypothesis
6.1
.
Denote by
\mu_{\mathcal{N}}
the law of a matrix whose entries are
independent complex centered Gaussian variables of variance
1/N
, and
by
\tilde{\nu}
the measure given by

\differential\tilde{\nu}(C)=\frac{(\det C)^{-1/2}}{\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}(\det C^{\prime})^{-1/2}\differential\nu(C^{\prime})}\differential\nu(C).

Then, for any measurable function

f\colon{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
, we have

\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}f(X)\differential\mu_{N,M}(X)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}f(G\sqrt{C})\differential\mu_{\mathcal{N}}(G)\differential\tilde{\nu}(C).

Lemma
6.3
implies that it is equivalent to
consider a random matrix of law
\mu_{N,M}
— a Gaussian matrix with random
covariance — or the product of two independent matrices: a standard
Gaussian matrix
X
and a positive-definite matrix
C^{1/2}
with
C

of law
\nu
.

Proof.

We perform the change of variables
X^{\prime}=X\sqrt{C^{-1}}
whose Jacobian
is
(\det C)^{-1/2}
. We get that

\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}f(X)\differential\mu_{N,M}(X)=\frac{1}{Z_{N,\nu}}\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}f(X\sqrt{C})\mathrm{e}^{-N\Tr XX^{\dagger}}(\det C)^{-1/2}\differential X\differential\mu(C),

and

Z_{N,\nu}=\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}\int_{{\rm M}_{N\times M}(\mathbb{C})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\Tr XX^{\dagger}}(\det C)^{-1/2}\differential X\differential\nu(C)=\Bigl(\frac{2\pi}{N}\Bigr)^{MN}\int_{\mathcal{P}(M)}(\det C)^{-1/2}\differential\nu(C).

This yields the result as

\differential\mu_{\mathcal{N}}(G)=\Bigl(\frac{2\pi}{N}\Bigr)^{-MN}\exp\Bigl(-N\Tr GG^{\dagger}\Bigr)\differential G.\qed

When computing moments of
XX^{\dagger}
, we have that

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\sigma}(XX^{\dagger})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\sigma}(GCG^{\dagger})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\sigma}(CG^{\dagger}G)\Bigr],

and hence it is equivalent to compute the moment of the Wishart matrix
with random covariance
XX^{\dagger}
or the moments of
CG^{\dagger}G
– where

G^{\dagger}G
is a usual Wishart matrix.

6.2
The random tensor case

Fix an integer
D\geq 1
, the number of inputs (or colors), and dimensions

N_{1},\ldots,N_{D}\geq 1
. Let

A=(A_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{D};j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}})
be a random tensor with
2D
indices of law
\nu
.
As detailed in Sec.
3.1
, the tensor
A
can be seen as a square matrix
A_{(1_{D})}
of dimension
\prod_{c=1}^{D}N_{c}
and a tensor
T
can be seen as a vector of length
\prod_{c=1}^{D}N_{c}
. In this section, we let

N=\max_{1\leq c\leq D}N_{c}
.

Motivated by the discussion of Section

6.1
, we make the following
assumptions.

Hypothesis 6.4
.

1.

The random matrix
A_{(1_{D})}
obtained by grouping the indices is almost-surely a
Hermitian positive-definite matrix;

2.

The laplace transform of
A^{-1}
,

\Lambda_{A}\colon J\mapsto\int_{\mathcal{P}(\prod_{c}N_{c})}\mathrm{e}^{-\left<A^{-1}_{(1_{D})},J\otimes\bar{J}\right>_{{\rm HS}}}\ \differential\nu(A)

is well-defined for all
J\in\mathbb{C}^{\prod_{c}N_{c}}
and
decays faster than any polynomial in the coefficients of
J

as
\|J\|_{\infty}=\max_{\bm{i}}|J_{\bm{i}}|\to\infty
.

These hypotheses allow us to define the law of a Gaussian tensor with random covariance
A
.

Definition 6.5

(Gaussian tensor with random covariance
T_{A}
)
.

Let
A
be a random tensor which satisfies Hypothesis
6.4
. We define the measure
\mu_{\bm{N}}
by

\mu_{\bm{N}}(T_{A})=\frac{1}{Z_{\bm{N},\nu}}\int_{\mathcal{P}(N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d})}\mathrm{e}^{-N^{D-1}T_{A}\cdot A^{-1}\cdot\bar{T}_{A}}\differential\nu(A)\differential T_{A}=\frac{1}{Z_{\bm{N},\nu}}\Lambda_{A}\Bigl(N^{(D-1)/2}T_{A}\Bigr)\differential T_{A},

(175)

where the partition function
Z_{\bm{N},\nu}
is

Z_{\bm{N},\nu}=\int_{\mathbb{C}^{N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}(N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d})}\mathrm{e}^{-N^{D-1}T_{A}\cdot A^{-1}\cdot\bar{T}_{A}}\differential\nu(A)\differential T_{A}.

We denote a random tensor with law
\mu_{\bm{N}}
by
T_{A}
.

Hypothesis
6.4
ensures that the
moments of
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
are finite.

Remark 6.6

(Reduction to the tensor HCIZ integral)
.

If
A=U\cdot A^{\prime}\cdot U^{\dagger}
with
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D}
with
U_{c}\in\mathbb{U}(N_{c}),1\leq c\leq D
and
A^{\prime}
a deterministic tensor, then we get that
\Lambda_{A}
is the tensor HCIZ integral studied in
[
CGL23
,
CGL23a
]
.

Proceeding as in the matrix case and using the change of variable

T^{\prime}=\bar{A}_{(1_{D})}^{-1/2}\cdot T
, where
A_{(1_{D})}^{-1/2}
is the square root
of the positive-definite matrix
A_{(1_{D})}^{-1}
. We get the following result.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume that
A
satisfies Hypothesis
6.4
. Denote by

\mu^{\mathcal{N}}_{\bm{N}}
the law of a tensor whose entries are
independent complex centered normal variables of variance
N^{1-D}
,
and by
\tilde{\nu}
the measure given by

\differential\tilde{\nu}(A)=\frac{(\det A_{(1_{D})})^{-1/2}}{\int_{\mathcal{P}(N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d})}(\det A^{\prime}_{(1_{D})})^{-1/2}\differential\nu(A^{\prime})}\differential\nu(A).

Then, for any measurable function

f\colon\mathbb{C}^{N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d}}\to\mathbb{C}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{C}^{N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d}}}f(T_{A})\differential\mu_{\bm{N}}(T_{A})=\int_{\mathcal{P}(N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d})}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{N_{1}\times\cdots\times N_{d}}}f(\bar{A}^{1/2}\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}})\differential\mu^{\mathcal{N}}_{\bm{N}}(T_{\mathbbm{1}})\differential\tilde{\nu}(A).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
6.3
. By
performing the change of variable
T^{\prime}=\bar{A}^{-1/2}\cdot T
, whose
Jacobian is
\det A_{(1_{D})}^{-1/2}
, we get the result.
∎

The computation of expectation of trace-invariants can be performed using
Lemma
6.7
. We have

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\overline{A^{1/2}}\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}},A^{1/2}\cdot\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})\Bigr],

(176)

where
A
has law
\tilde{\nu}
and
T_{\mathbbm{1}}
is a standard Gaussian tensor.

Remark 6.8

(Reduction to the matrix case)
.

In the case
D=2
and
A=\Id\otimes C
where
C
is
a random positive definite matrix, we recover the matrix result. Indeed, we then get

A_{i_{1}i_{2};j_{1}j_{2}}=\delta_{i_{1},j_{1}}C_{i_{2}j_{2}}

and

A^{-1}=\Id\otimes C^{-1}.

Hence, for any pure tensor
T
with
2
indices (or colors)

\langle A^{-1},T\otimes\bar{T}\rangle_{{\rm HS}}=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{N}\delta_{i_{1},j_{1}}\Bigl(C^{-1}\Bigr)_{i_{2}j_{2}}T_{i_{1}i_{2}}\bar{T}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{N}T_{ki}\bar{T}_{kj}\Bigl(C^{-1}\Bigr)_{ij}.

This means that under
\mu
, the tensor
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
is distributed like a Gaussian matrix with random covariance
C
.

As with any Gaussian family of random variables, we may use Wick
formula to compute joint moments of entries of a random tensor
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})

with law
\mu_{\bm{N}}
given in (
175
).

Lemma 6.9

(Wick formula)
.

Let
T_{A}
be a random Gaussian tensor with a covariance
A
that
satisfies Hypothesis
6.4
. Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}

and
\bm{i},\bm{j}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}
. We have

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\prod_{k=1}^{n}(T_{A})_{\bm{i}(k)}(\bar{T}_{A})_{\bm{j}(k)}\Bigr]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\prod_{k=1}^{n}A_{\bm{i}(k);\bm{j}(k)}\Bigr].

Furthermore, we have for any
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\overline{T_{A}})\Bigr]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}(A)\Bigr].

Proof.

Conditionally on
A
, the family
T_{A}=\Bigl((T_{A})_{\bm{i}}\Bigr)_{\bm{i}\in[N]^{D}}
is a
Gaussian family with covariance
N^{1-D}A
. We get by the
Wick formula,

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\prod_{k=1}^{n}(T_{A})_{\bm{i}(k)}(\bar{T}_{A})_{\bm{j}(k)}\mid A\Bigr]=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\prod_{k=1}^{n}\mathds{E}\Bigl[(T_{A})_{\bm{i}(k)}(\bar{T}_{A})_{\bm{j}(\eta(k))}\mid A\Bigr].

The covariance is then given by

\mathds{E}\Bigl[(T_{A})_{\bm{i}(k)}(\bar{T}_{A})_{\bm{j}(\eta(k))}\mid A\Bigr]=N^{1-D}A_{\bm{i}(k);\bm{j}(\eta(k))}.

Taking the expectation with respect to
A
gives the first result.

To get the second claim, we only notice that

\begin{split}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\overline{T_{A}})\Bigr]&=\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[(T_{A})_{\bm{i}(k)}(\bar{T}_{A})_{\bm{j}(\eta(k))}\Bigr]\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{k=1}^{N}\delta_{\bm{i}(k)_{c},\bm{j}(\sigma_{c}(k))_{c}}\\
&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}(A)\Bigr].\end{split}

This is the second claim.
∎

We finally note that a random Gaussian tensor with random covariance

A
can be expressed as a product of
A
with a standard random
Gaussian tensor.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
(T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
be a standard Gaussian tensor, i.e. a Gaussian tensor
with covariance identity, and
T_{A}
be a Gaussian tensor with
covariance
A
that satisfies Hypothesis
6.4
. Then, we have for all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\overline{T_{A}})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}},\overline{T_{\mathbbm{1}}})\Bigr]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}(A)\Bigr].

Remark 6.11

(Non-positive or non-full rank covariances)
.

Lemma
6.7
leads us to interpret the random tensor
B\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}}
for
any random mixed tensor
B
and standard Gaussian tensor
T_{\mathbbm{1}}

as being a Gaussian tensor with formal covariance
BB^{\dagger}
, a tensor
that may not be a full-rank as a matrix of dimension
\prod_{c}N_{c}
.

In fact, in view of Lemma
6.10
, a generalization of the Gaussian
tensor with random covariance
A
is provided by

(A\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
. Indeed, this tensor always makes sens
whether
A
is positive-definite or not. We will study such tensors
starting from Section
7
.

Proof.

The tensor
T_{A}
is a Gaussian family with covariance
A
. Wick
formula – Lemma
6.9
– implies that for all
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\overline{T_{A}})\Bigr]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}(A)\Bigr].

On the other hand,

\begin{split}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}},\overline{T_{\mathbbm{1}}})\Bigr]&=\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\colon[n]\to[N]}\mathds{E}\Bigl[A_{\bm{i};\bm{j}}\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[(T_{\mathbbm{1}}^{\otimes n})_{\bm{j}}(\overline{T_{\mathbbm{1}}})^{\otimes n}_{\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma}^{-1}}\Bigr]\\
&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\colon[n]\to[N]}\mathds{E}\Bigl[A_{\bm{i};\bm{j}}\Bigr]\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\delta_{\bm{j},\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\circ\eta}\\
&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\eta\bm{\sigma}}(A)\Bigr].\end{split}

The invariance by conjugation of
\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A)
gives

\Tr_{\eta\bm{\sigma}}(A)=\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}(A)
and hence

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A\cdot T_{\mathbbm{1}},\overline{T_{\mathbbm{1}}})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\overline{T_{A}})\Bigr].\qed

6.3
Finite size precursors

The quantities whose
N\to\infty
limits define the asymptotic cumulants and free cumulants are
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]
and
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]
. Here we express them in terms of the analogous quantities for
A
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
A
a random mixed
tensor satisfying Hypothesis
6.4
, and

(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
with law
\mu_{N}
(see
(
175
)). For any
N\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}[A]\\
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}[A]\\
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}[A].\end{split}

(177)

Proof.

We start by proving the first claim on
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]
, we have by definition

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}).

Using Lemma
6.9
and changing variables from
\bm{\rho}\eta
to

\bm{\rho}
, we get

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}\eta}[A]\Bigr]{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}\eta}[A].

To prove the second claim on
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]
,
one starts from the classical cumulant-moment formula and use Lemma

6.9
:

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\prod_{B\in\Pi_{[n]}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}|_{B}}(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})\Bigr]\\
&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\eta)\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\prod_{B\in\Pi_{[n]}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1}|_{B}}(A)\Bigr].\end{split}

By exchanging the sums and using the bijection
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\simeq\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
, we get

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\prod_{B\in\Pi_{[n]}}\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1}|_{B}}(A)\right]\\
&=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)}\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\pi}\prod_{B\in\pi}\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1}|_{B}}(A)\right].\end{split}

The second claim immediately follows.

Finally, the third claim on
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]
is
obtained through the same transformation from a pure to a regular
partition:

N^{n(D-1)}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi_{[n]},{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}[A]=\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\eta^{-1})\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\pi}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\pi,{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}[A].\qed

6.4
Asymptotics and tensorial free cumulants of arbitrary order

Moments

The large
N
factorization property has been discussed in (
167
). From (
37
), the moment associated to
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
of the standard Gaussian
(T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
scales as
n-\delta^{\bullet}({\bm{\sigma}})
, defined in (
43
).

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
A
a random mixed
tensor satisfying Hypothesis
6.4
, and

(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
with law
\mu_{N}
(see

175
). Assume that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}{N^{-\#\bm{\sigma}}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(A)\Bigr]=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)\;.

Then the moments of
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
have the following asymptotic scaling in
N
and large
N
limit:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{\delta^{\bullet}({\bm{\sigma}})-n}\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta^{\bullet}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}),{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a)\;.

(178)

In particular,
{\bm{\sigma}}
factorizes for
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
if and only if it does for the standard Gaussian
(T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
.

Proof.

From Lemma
6.10
and the hypothesis of the present lemma, one has:

\mathds{E}\left[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})\right]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{\sum_{c=1}^{D}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}),{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a)+o(1)\bigr)\;.

(179)

One has large
N
factorization for
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
if and only if the
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
such that
d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta^{\bullet}({\bm{\sigma}})
satisfy
\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}(\eta)\leq\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}})
, which is also the necessary and sufficient condition for the large
N
factorization of
{\bm{\sigma}}
for
(T_{\mathbbm{1}},\bar{T}_{\mathbbm{1}})
.
∎

Case where the covariance is deterministic or uniform in a LU orbit.

Let
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. We recall the notions introduced in (
161
):

\begin{split}\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})&=\min_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}(2K({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1})+d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta))\;,\\
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})&=\bigl\{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},\ 2K({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1})+d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})\bigr\}\;.\end{split}

Theorem 6.14
.

Consider a mixed deterministic tensor
A^{\prime}
with
D
inputs and such that for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-\#\bm{\sigma}}\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A^{\prime})<\infty
. If
A=A^{\prime}
or
A=UA^{\prime}U^{\dagger}
with
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{D}
,
U_{c}
Haar distributed, one has for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
:

\displaystyle\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]

\displaystyle=N^{n-\delta({\bm{\sigma}})}\bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})+o(1)\bigr)\;,

(180)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]

\displaystyle=N^{2-\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})+n(1-D)}\bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})+o(1)\bigr)\;.

(181)

and the asymptotics cumulants and tensorial free cumulants of
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
are expressed as:

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{K_{p}({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=1}\\
{d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a^{\prime})

(182)

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a^{\prime})\;.

(183)

Proof.

With these assumptions, one has from (
177
) and (
32
)

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1})=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(A^{\prime})\;,

(184)

which together with (
129
) imply (
180
) and (
182
). On the other hand, from (
177
) and (
76
):

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\mathcal{L}^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta}[A^{\prime}]

(185)

From Thm.
4.4
:

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]=N^{n(1-D)}\sum_{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}N^{2(1-K({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}))+\sum_{c}\#(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1})-nD}\lambda^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a^{\prime})(1+o(1))\;,

(186)

which imply (
181
) and (
183
).
∎

If
A^{\prime}=\mathbbm{1}
, the
N^{D}\times N^{D}
identity matrix, the values of
\varphi{\rm m}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(1)
and
\lambda_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}^{\rm m}(1)
are given (
145
). We recover as expected the value of
\varphi^{\rm p}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\bar{t}_{1})
given in Sec.
2.6
, Eq. (
44
), and for
{\bm{\sigma}}
connected, the value of
\kappa^{\rm p}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\bar{t}_{1})
given in Sec. 6.1 of
[
CGL25
]
:

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\bar{t}_{1})=\#\bigl\{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\ \mid\ d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta({\bm{\sigma}})\bigr\}\;,\qquad\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{1},\bar{t}_{1})=\delta_{n,1}\;.

(187)

General case of a random covariance with the matrix product scaling.

Theorem 6.15
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
A
a random mixed
tensor satisfying Hypothesis
6.4
as well as the matrix product scaling hypothesis (
128
), and

(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
with law
\mu_{N}
(see

175
). Then:

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{2+n-\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})+o(1)\Bigr)\\
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}[T_{A},\bar{T}_{A}]&=N^{2+n(1-D)-\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})+o(1)\Bigr)\;,\end{split}

(188)

and the asymptotics cumulants and tensorial free cumulants of
(T_{A},\bar{T}_{A})
are expressed in terms of those of
A
as:

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a)

(189)

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}(a)\;.

(190)

The proof is analogous to that of (
181
) and (
183
).

Comments on the scaling and connectivity.

Consider a deterministic mixed tensor
A^{\prime}
such that for every
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-\#\bm{\sigma}}\Tr_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(A^{\prime})<\infty
. We have shown that under Hypothesis
6.4
(that guarantees that the definition is well-defined), a pure Gaussian random tensor whose covariance is either
A^{\prime}
, or taken uniformly at random in the LU orbit of
A^{\prime}
, scales as a standard complex Gaussian tensor. Furthermore, (
182
) involves a connectivity condition which is due to the lack of randomness (in the sense that the classical cumulants of more than one connected trace-invariants on the mixed side vanish). The role of connexifying then falls down on the permutation
\eta
.

On the other hand, still under Hypothesis
6.4
but in the general case where the random covariance of
(T_{A},\bar{T_{A}})
is only assumed to scale as (
128
), there is no explicit connectivity condition in (
189
). To be more precise, this question depends on the elements of
\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})
(the
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}
that are solutions of
2K({\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1})+d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\varepsilon({\bm{\sigma}})
): if the only elements
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}({\bm{\sigma}})
are such that
\Pi_{\rm p}(\eta)\leq\Pi_{\rm p}({\bm{\sigma}})
, then the
\eta
do not connexify, and this role falls on
A
. From Prop.
5.8
, this is for instance the case for
{\bm{\sigma}}
melonic (the
{\bm{\sigma}}
that scale the strongest when the number of pure connected components is fixed). In that case, the the additional randomness of
A
is responsible for the “boosted” fluctuations, discussed in Sec.
5.3.1
, see (
169
) and (
170
).

6.5
Example: tensor products of random matrices as covariance

The relations of Thm.
6.15
and Thm.
6.14
agree for
{\bm{\sigma}}
purely connected. In this section, we focus on the purely connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
, and for a covariance that can be written as a tensor product of matrices, one for each color
c\in[D]
.

Proposition 6.16
.

If
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
is purely connected and
A=A_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{D}
, where
A_{1},\ldots,A_{D}
are
N\times N
unitarily invariant random matrices behaving asymptotically as (
105
), one has:

\begin{split}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\ \varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}),\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}}(a_{c})\;,\\
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\\
{d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta({\bm{\sigma}})}\end{subarray}}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\ \kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}),\sigma_{c}\eta^{-1}}(a_{c})\;.\end{split}

(191)

Proof.

The first formula is a consequence of Lemma
4.2
, and the second of Lemma
4.7
.
∎

From Theorem
2.6
, purely connected melonic
{\bm{\sigma}}
have the property that there is a unique
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
such that
d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)=\delta({\bm{\sigma}})
. For every
D\geq 3
, there are many other tuples
{\bm{\sigma}}
satisfying this property. For such
{\bm{\sigma}}
, up to a relabeling, one can always choose this minimizer to be the identity,
\mathrm{id}_{n}
, and therefore:

\begin{split}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)\;,\\
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(a)\;.\end{split}

(192)

If in addition the hypothesis of Prop.
6.16
are satisfied, (
191
) simplifies to:

\begin{split}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\ \varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}),\sigma_{c}}(a_{c})\;,\\
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\ \kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\sigma_{c}),\sigma_{c}}(a_{c})\;.\end{split}

(193)

Example 6.17
.

If
{\bm{\sigma}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
is purely connected and
\eta=\mathrm{id}_{n}
is the only permutation minimizing
d({\bm{\sigma}},\eta)
, and if
A=A_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{D}
, with either
A_{c}
a Wishart random matrix of parameters
(N,r_{c}N)
, or
A_{c}=U_{c}A^{\prime}_{c}U_{c}^{\dagger}
with
U_{c}\in\mathbb{U}(N)
Haar distributed and
A^{\prime}c
a deterministic matrix whose spectrum is that of Wishart random matrix of parameters
(N,r_{c}N)
.
Such matrices have free cumulants given by
r_{c}
, see
[
MS17
]
.
We then have:

\begin{split}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\sum_{\rho_{c}\leq\sigma_{c}}r_{c}^{\#(\rho_{c})}\;,\\
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})&=\prod_{c=1}^{D}r_{c}^{\#(\sigma_{c})}\;.\end{split}

(194)

In the square case, that is, if for all
c\in[D]
,
r_{c}=1
, then
\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=1
.

If
\bm{\sigma}
is melonic and connected and
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{D}=r
, we have
\#{\bm{\sigma}}=1+n(D-1)
and

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{a},\bar{t}_{a})=r^{1+n(D-1)}

(195)

Written otherwise, if we rescale
A
by
r^{1-D}
, setting

\tilde{A}=r^{1-D}A
, we get

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(t_{\tilde{a}},\bar{t}_{\tilde{a}})=r.

(196)

This gives an asymptotic sequence of free cumulants reminiscent of the
free Poisson law of parameter
r
appearing in (random matrix) free probability.

Finally, we notice that such random tensor distribution can be realized
with a deterministic
A
: it suffices to take a tensor product of
deterministic matrices whose eigenvalues are the zeroes of an
appropriate Laguerre polynomial. Indeed, in the large dimension limit,
the eigenvalues of a Wishart matrices are well-approximated by zeroes of Laguerre polynomials, see for instance
[
Det02
]
and references therein.

This provides examples of distributions whose melonic tensorial free cumulants are one, which was a question raised in

[
CGL25
]
, Sec. 6.2. More generally, Gaussians with LU-invariant random covariances are the first examples of LU-invariant random tensors with no-trivial tensorial free cumulants (all explicit examples previously studied were global unitary invariant).

7
General formulae for products of tensors

Motivated by Lemmata
6.7
and

6.10
, and the discussion in Remark

6.11
, we study the product of a mixed and a pure
tensor:
(B\cdot T,\bar{T})
. It is also natural to consider the
product
A\cdot B
for
A
and
B
two mixed tensors. This section is
dedicated to give general formulae at finite
N
for the moments,
classical cumulants, precursors
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
, and finite-
N
precursors to the free
cumulants of such product tensors.

Notice that in general, we will not require that
B
is LU-invariant.
Indeed, it suffices that the tensors it is multiplied to are
LU-invariant to write general formulae.

7.1
Moments of products of tensors

We start by studying the moments of a tensor
(B\cdot T,\bar{T})
in the pure
case, and
B\cdot A
in the mixed case. Actually, we give a proof in
the more general case of families of tensors

(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})=((B^{(1)}\cdot T^{(1)},\bar{T}^{(1)}),\ldots,(B^{(l)}\cdot T^{(l)},\bar{T}^{(l)}))
and

\bm{B}\cdot\bm{A}=(B^{(1)}\cdot A^{(1)},\ldots,B^{(l)}\cdot A^{(l)})
.

Proposition 7.1

(Moments of product of tensors)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{B}=(B^{(1)},\ldots,B^{(n)})
be a family of random mixed tensor.

•

Let
\bm{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})
be a LU-invariant
family of mixed tensors independent from
\bm{B}
, and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, then

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{A})\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{A})\Bigr].

•

Let
\bm{T}=(T^{(1)},\ldots,T^{(n)})
be a LU-invariant
family of pure tensors independent from
\bm{B}
, and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. We have

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr].

Proof.

We prove the result in the pure case, the computation is similar in
the mixed case. The invariance by conjugation by local-unitary elements yields

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]=\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot U\cdot\bm{T},\bar{U}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr],

where
U=U_{1}\otimes\cdots U_{D}\in\mathbb{U}(N)^{\otimes D}
is
Haar-distributed. By expanding the trace-invariant and using the independence,
we get

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{j^{\prime}},\bm{k},\bm{k^{\prime}}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{B}_{\bm{j^{\prime}}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{j}}\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[(U^{\otimes n})_{\bm{j},\bm{k}}(\bar{U}^{\otimes n})_{\bm{j^{\prime}},\bm{k^{\prime}}}\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{T}_{\bm{k}}\overline{\bm{T}}_{\bm{k^{\prime}}}\Bigr],

where we recall that we use the notation

\bm{B}_{\bm{i};\bm{j}}=\prod_{k=1}^{n}B^{(k)}_{\bm{i}(k);\bm{j}(k)}
and

\bm{T}_{\bm{i}}=\prod_{k=1}^{n}T^{(k)}_{\bm{i}(k)}
.

The Weingarten formula — Theorem
2.10
—
allows us to integrate over the unitary variables

\begin{split}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\ \sum_{\bm{j},\bm{j^{\prime}},\bm{k},\bm{k^{\prime}}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}\delta_{\bm{j},\bm{j^{\prime}}\circ\bm{\rho}}\delta_{\bm{k},\bm{k^{\prime}}\circ\bm{\tau}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{B}_{\bm{j^{\prime}}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{j}}\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{T}_{\bm{k}}\overline{\bm{T}}_{\bm{k^{\prime}}}\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}^{-1}\bm{\tau})\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\ \sum_{\bm{j},\bm{k}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{B}_{\bm{j}\circ\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1};\bm{j}}\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[\bm{T}_{\bm{k}}\overline{\bm{T}}_{\bm{k}\circ\bm{\tau}^{-1}}\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}^{-1}\bm{\tau})\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma\rho^{-1}}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}^{-1}\bm{\tau}).\end{split}

The invariance by conjugation of the Weingarten function

{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}
allows us to conclude:

\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr].\qed

7.2
Classical cumulants for products of tensors

Let us now give formulae to express the classical cumulants for
products of tensors. Actually, we give formulae for a more general
version of the cumulants defined as follows. Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. Given a family of
n
mixed tensors

\bm{A}
and
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(n)
, we set in the mixed
case

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\pi_{1};\bm{A}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\leq\pi\leq\pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Biggl(\prod_{S\in\pi_{2}}\mu_{\pi|_{S}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}|_{S}}(\bm{A})\Bigr]\Biggr).

(197)

Given a family of
n
pure tensors
(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})
and

\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
, we set in the pure case

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Biggl(\prod_{S\in\Pi_{2}}\mu_{\Pi|_{S}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}|_{S}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]\Biggr).

(198)

The formulae for
\pi_{1}\neq 0_{n}
and
\Pi_{1}\neq 0_{n,\bar{n}}

are provided for the sake of completeness: for instance, if

\bm{\sigma^{(1)}},\ldots,\bm{\sigma^{(K)}}
are the connected components of

\bm{\sigma}
(in the sense that
\bm{\sigma}=\bm{\sigma^{(1)}}\cdots\bm{\sigma^{(K)}}
and

K(\bm{\sigma^{(p)}})=1
), having
\pi_{1}
or
\Pi_{1}
non-zero and coarser than
\Pi(\bm{\sigma})
or
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
means that
we consider cumulants of products of trace-invariants associated to
the
\bm{\sigma^{(p)}}
’s. We give later a similar generalizations of the
finite-
N
precursors to the free cumulants.

Proposition 7.2

(Classical cumulants for products of tensors)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
, and
\bm{B}=(B^{(1)},\ldots,B^{(n)})
be a family of random mixed tensors.

•

Let
\bm{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})
be a LU-invariant
family of mixed tensors independent from
\bm{B}
,

\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(n)
, and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, then

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl[\pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{A}\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi_{2}\\
\Pi(\bm{\tau})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\leq\pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{B}]\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[\bm{A}]\\
&\quad\quad\times\prod_{S\in\pi_{2}}{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}|_{S},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}|_{S}\Bigr].\end{split}

•

Let

(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})=\Bigl((T^{(1)},\bar{T}^{(1)}),\ldots,(T^{(n)},\bar{T}^{(n)})\Bigr)

be a LU-invariant family of pure tensors independent from

\bm{B}
,
\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
, and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. We have

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}\Bigr]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\leq(\Pi_{2})_{[n]}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{B}]\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[\bm{T},\bar{\bm{T}}]\\
&\quad\quad\times\prod_{S\in\Pi_{2}}{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}|_{S},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}|_{S}\Bigr].\end{split}

Proof.

We give the proof for pure case, the mixed case being very similar.
We consider the case
\Pi_{2}=1_{n,\bar{n}}
. The general case is
obtained by taking a product of cumulants of this form. Proposition

7.1
gives

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{1_{n,\bar{n}},\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}\Bigr]&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\prod_{S\in\Pi_{[n]}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}|_{S}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi_{[n]}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\prod_{S\in\Pi_{[n]}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}|_{S}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}|_{S})\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}|_{S}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr].\end{split}

We rewrite the two expectations as products of classical cumulants and get

\begin{split}&\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{1_{n,\bar{n}},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]\\
&\quad=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi,\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}\leq\Pi\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\Phi_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{B}]\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]\prod_{S\in\Pi_{[n]}}{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}|_{S})\\
&\quad=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}\Phi_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{B}]\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}].\qed\end{split}

7.3
Quantities
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
for products of tensors

We can slightly modify Proposition
7.1
to treat
the quantities
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}
in the mixed and pure case.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{B}=(B^{(1)},\ldots,B^{(n)})
be a
family of random mixed tensors.

•

Let
\bm{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})
be a LU-invariant family of
random mixed tensors independent from
\bm{B}
, and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. We have

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot\bm{A}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{A}].

•

Let
(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})=\Bigl((T^{(1)},\bar{T}^{(1)}),\ldots,(T^{(n)},\bar{T}^{(n)})\Bigr)
be a LU-invariant
family of random pure tensors independent from
\bm{B}
. We have

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].

Proof.

We only do the proof in the pure case as the proof in the
mixed case is very similar. Using Proposition
7.1
, we have

\begin{split}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]&=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\rho}}(\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau},\bm{\mu}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\tau}\bm{\mu}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})\Bigr]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}).\end{split}

Using the definition of
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
, this can be rewritten as

\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n,\bar{n}}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\tau}^{-1}\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}).

We perform the change of variable
\bm{\rho}^{\prime}=\bm{\tau}^{-1}\bm{\rho}
and get

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\mathds{E}\Bigl[\Tr_{\bm{\tau}}(\bm{B})\Bigr]\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]{\rm W}_{\bm{N}}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\bm{\tau})=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].\qed

7.4
Finite-
N
precursors to the free cumulants for products of tensors

We use the extension of the finite-
N
precursors to the free
cumulants defined as follows. In the mixed case, given

\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(n)
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
,
we set

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\pi_{1};\bm{A}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\leq\pi\leq\pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\pi}\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\pi,\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{A}].

(199)

In the pure case, given
\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)

and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, we set

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{n}\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\Pi}\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi,\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].

(200)

Notice that we may replace
\pi_{1}
by
\pi_{1}\vee\pi(\bm{\sigma})

(respectively
\Pi_{1}
by
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
) in the expression
above without changing their value.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{B}=(B^{(1)},\ldots,B^{(n)})
be a
family of random mixed tensors.

•

Let
\bm{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})
be a LU-invariant
family of random mixed tensors independent from
\bm{B}
,

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and

\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(n)
. We have

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{A}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\pi_{1}\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{A}].\end{split}

•

Let

(\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}})=\Bigl((T^{(1)},\bar{T}^{(1)}),\ldots,(T^{(n)},\bar{T}^{(n)})\Bigr)

be a LU-invariant family of random pure tensors independent
from
\bm{B}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and

\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)
. We have

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\ \mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[T,\bar{T}].\end{split}

Proof.

We give the proof in the pure case only, as the mixed case and
second pure case are very similar. The finite-
N
precursors of the
free cumulant

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma},\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}}[\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]

can be written as

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\prod_{S\in\Pi_{2}}\mu_{\Pi|_{S}}\Bigr)\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi,\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].

We use Proposition
7.3
to get

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\Pi_{p}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\prod_{S\in\Pi_{2}}\mu_{\Pi|_{S}}\Bigr)\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi_{[n]},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].

We can then rewrite the quantities
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
in terms of finite-
N
precursors of the free
cumulants using (
73
) and (
80
):

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi_{[n]},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\leq\Pi_{[n]}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\quad\text{ and }\quad\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\leq\Pi\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].

Notice that

\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})
.
Hence, after exchanging the sums, the sum on
\Pi
becomes

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}\leq\Pi\leq\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\prod_{S\in\Pi_{2}}\mu_{\Pi|_{S}}\Bigr)=\delta_{\Pi_{1}\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime},\Pi_{2}},

where we used Remark
2.5
. We get

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\sigma}}[\Pi_{1};\bm{B}\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\Pi_{1}\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=\Pi_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[\bm{B}]\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].\qed

7.5
The product of tensor
(B_{1}\cdot T,\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\bar{T})

In the pure case, a natural product of tensor is to consider

(B_{1}\cdot T,\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\bar{T})
, where
(T,\bar{T})
is a pure
tensor and
B
and
B^{\prime}
are mixed tensor. Whenever both
B_{1}
and
B_{2}

are tensor products of random matrices, we recover the case discussed
in the preceding sections.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. Let

(\bm{T},\bm{\bar{T}})=\Bigl((T^{(1)},\bar{T}^{(1)}),\ldots,(T^{(n)},\bar{T}^{(n)})\Bigr)

be a pure tensor, and
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
be two mixed tensors, with
B_{1}
a tensor product of random matrices. We have

\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(B_{1}\cdot\bm{T},\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}})=\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl((B_{2}^{\dagger}B_{1})\cdot\overline{\bm{T}},\bm{T}\Bigr).

Furthermore, we have

\begin{split}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B_{1}\cdot T,\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\bar{T}]&=\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[(B_{2}^{\dagger}B_{1})\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]\\
\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B_{1}\cdot\bm{T},\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}}]&=\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[(B_{2}^{\dagger}B_{1})\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]\\
\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B_{1}\cdot\bm{T},\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}}]&=\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[(B_{2}^{\dagger}B_{1})\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}].\end{split}

Proof.

Let us prove the first claim. We have

\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(B_{1}\cdot\bm{T},\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}})=\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}(B_{1})_{\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{j}}(\bar{B}_{2})_{\bm{i};\bm{k}}\bm{T}_{\bm{j}}\overline{\bm{T}}_{\bm{k}}.

Since
B_{1}=B_{1}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes B_{1}^{(D)}
, we have

(\bar{B}_{2})_{\bm{i};\bm{k}}=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{p=1}^{n}(\bar{B}_{2}^{(c)})_{\bm{i}_{c}(p);\bm{k}_{c}(p)}=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{p=1}^{n}(\bar{B}_{2}^{(c)})_{\bm{i}_{c}\circ\sigma_{c}(p);\bm{k}_{c}\circ\sigma_{c}(p)}=(B_{2}^{\dagger})_{\bm{k}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{i}\circ\bm{\sigma}}.

This allows us to sum on
\bm{i}
and get the first claim:

\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}(B_{1}\cdot\bm{T},\bar{B}_{2}\cdot\overline{\bm{T}})=\sum_{\bm{j},\bm{k}\colon[n]\to[N]^{D}}(B_{2}^{\dagger}\cdot B_{1})_{\bm{k}\circ\bm{\sigma};\bm{j}}\bm{T}_{\bm{j}}\bm{\bar{T}}_{\bm{k}}=\Tr_{\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl((B_{2}^{\dagger}\cdot B_{1})\cdot\overline{\bm{T}},\bm{T}\Bigr).

The second claim is then a direct consequence of definitions
(
61
), (
34
), and
(
77
).
∎

8
Asymptotics for products of tensors

When considering random matrices, and free variables obtained as

N\to\infty
limits of such objects, the free cumulants of products
of free variables satisfy remarkable relations. An example is the
following theorem.

Theorem 8.1

(Restatement of
[
NS06
, Theorem 14.4]
)
.

Let
a
and
b
be two free variables. For all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
, and

\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
with
K(\sigma)=1
,

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{G}(\sigma)}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\tau}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\sigma\tau^{-1}}(a).

Remark 8.2
.

Theorem
8.1
is stated using
slightly different object that in
[
NS06
]
. Let us explain the
correspondence. Let
\gamma=(1\,\ldots\,n)
. Given a permutation

\sigma
with one cycle, i.e. with
K(\sigma)=1
, and
\mu
such that
\gamma=\mu\sigma\mu^{-1}
, there is a bijection
between the sets of non-crossing partitions on
n
elements
\mathsf{NC}(n)

and the set
\mathbb{G}(\sigma)
given by

\begin{cases}\mathbb{G}(\sigma)&\to\mathsf{NC}(n)\\
\rho&\mapsto\Pi(\mu\rho\mu^{-1}).\end{cases}

Through this bijection,
\sigma\tau^{-1}
is then sent to the Kreweras
complement of
\Pi(\mu\tau\mu^{-1})
.

We will see in the sequel (in Theorems
8.9
and
8.11
) analogue formulae to Theorem

8.1
for the free cumulants of
products of tensors.

8.1
Classical cumulants for products of tensors

To describe the asymptotics of the classical cumulants for products of
tensors, we use pairs of forests of permutations, together with an additional
condition, which we now introduce

Definition 8.3

(Intertwined pairs of forests of permutations)
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
. A pair of mixed
forest of permutations
(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
and

(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
is said to be

intertwined
if

L\Bigl(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\pi_{2}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\Bigr)=0.

We denote by
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
the set of such pairs
\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\Bigr)
.

Similarly, given
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, a pair of a pure and a mixed
forest of permutations

(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)

and
(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
is said
to be
intertwined
if

L\biggl(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\Bigl(\Pi_{2}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)_{[n]};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\biggr)=0.

We denote by
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
the set of such pairs
\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)
. The set of intertwined pure pairs is then

\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=\Biggl\{\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)\colon\exists\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\cap\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}),\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Biggr\}.

Finally, the set of intertwined Gaussian pairs is

\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})=\Biggl\{\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)\colon\exists\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\cap\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}),\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Biggr\}.

Remark 8.4
.

The sets
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}},\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}
, and
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}
are non-empty. Indeed, if we let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
and take

\bm{\rho}=\bm{\mathrm{id}_{n}}
,
\pi_{1}=0_{n}
,

\bm{\tau}=\bm{\sigma}
,
\pi_{2}=\mathcal{P}(\bm{\sigma})
, and
\Pi_{2}=\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
, we get that

\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}),\quad\text{ and }\quad\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\cap\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma}).

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, and
A,B
be two
independent random mixed tensor with
A
LU-invariant. We assume
that their classical cumulants satisfy (
128
). Then the classical cumulants of
B\cdot A
also satisfies (
128
) and we have:

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot A]=N^{2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)+o(1)\Bigr)

with

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{\bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\rho}}(b)\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(a)\Gamma\Bigl[\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr].

Note that the sum appearing in the definition of
\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)
is non-trivial by Remark
8.4
.

Remark 8.6

(Connected
\bm{\sigma}
in the mixed case)
.

If
\bm{\sigma}
is connected, i.e.
K(\bm{\sigma})=1
, the condition

L(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\pi_{2}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}))=0

simplifies and becomes
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
. We get that

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\\
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\rho}}(b)\mathsf{M}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}).

Furthermore,
(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
and
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
, are equivalent to
\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
,
\pi_{1}=\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
. We get

\begin{split}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)&=\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\rho}),\bm{\rho}}(b)\mathsf{M}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1})\\
&=\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2}\bm{\tau}}(a)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}}(b).\end{split}

Proof.

Proposition
7.2
gives us

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot A]=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\\
\Pi(\bm{\tau})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]\Phi_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[A]{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr].

Using (
128
) and Lemma
4.5
we have

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]=N^{2(\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})+d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})+nD-\alpha_{1}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}(b)+o(1)\Bigr)

where
\alpha_{1}\geq 0
with equality if and only if
(\bm{\rho},\pi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
. Similarly, we have

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[A]=N^{2(\#\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}+d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\sigma})-\alpha_{2}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr),

where
\alpha_{2}\geq 0
with equality if and only if

(\bm{\tau},\pi^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
.
Finally, we have by Theorem
2.14

\begin{split}{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi(\bm{\sigma})&\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]\\
&=N^{2(1-\#\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime})-nD-d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})}\Bigl(\Gamma\Bigl[\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]+o(1)\Bigr).\end{split}

Putting everything together, we get that the product

\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]\Phi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[A]{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]

is of order

N^{2(1+\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})-K(\bm{\sigma})-\#\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime})+\#\bm{\sigma}-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}}.

We notice that since
\Pi(\bm{\tau})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}
we have

\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}=\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}
and

\begin{split}-2L(\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\vee\pi^{\prime},&\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi_{2};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}))\\
&=2\#\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\vee\pi^{\prime}+2\#\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}-2\#\Pi(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}-2K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}).\end{split}

This quantity is non-positive. We thus get that the power of
N
is at most

2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}

with equality if and only if
\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi^{\prime}),(\bm{\tau},\pi^{\prime\prime})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
. Note that by Remark
8.4
, this set is non-empty.
∎

Proposition 8.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
,
\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
,
(T,\bar{T})

be a LU-invariant random pure tensor that satisfies either the
scaling assumption (
165
) for pure tensors or the
Gaussian scaling assumption (
160
), and
B
a
random mixed tensor independent from
(T,\bar{T})
, that satisfies
the scaling assumption (
128
) for mixed tensors.
Then,
(B\cdot T,\bar{T})
satisfies the scaling assumption
(
165
):

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot T,\bar{T}]=N^{n+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr)

with

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\rho}}(b)\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(a)\Gamma\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{2},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]

if
(T,\bar{T})
satisfies (
165
), and

\varphi^{{\rm p}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\rho}}(b)\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(a)\Gamma\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{2},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]

if
(T,\bar{T})
satisfies (
160
).

Note that the sums appearing in the two definitions of

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})
are non-trivial by Remark

8.4
.

Remark 8.8

(Connected
\bm{\sigma}
in the pure case)
.

If
\bm{\sigma}
is purely connected, i.e.
K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1
, the condition
L(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\Bigl(\Pi_{2}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigr)_{[n]};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}))=0

simplifies and becomes
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
. We get that

\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})=\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\\
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\end{subarray}}\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\rho}}(b)\mathsf{M}(\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}).

Furthermore,
(\bm{\rho},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
and
\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
, are equivalent to
\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
,
\pi_{1}=\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
. We get

\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{(\bm{\tau},\Pi_{2})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})}\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}}(b).

Proof.

Proposition
7.2
immediately gives

\begin{split}\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot\bm{T},\overline{\bm{T}}]&=\sum_{\bm{\rho},\bm{\tau}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}\Phi_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[\bm{T},\bar{\bm{T}}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr].\end{split}

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau})
. Note that we have in particular

\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau},\eta)\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}
. When considering the classical
cumulant of
B
, the scaling assumption (
128
) and Lemma
4.5
give

\Phi_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]=N^{2(\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})+d(\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})+nD-\alpha_{1}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}(b)+o(1)\Bigr)

where
\alpha_{1}\geq 0
with equality if and only if

(\bm{\rho},\pi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})
.
Similarly, in the pure case, we use the scaling assumption (
165
) and Lemma

5.13
to get

\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[T,\bar{T}]=N^{n+2(\#\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})-\alpha_{2}}\Bigl(\varphi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr),

where
\alpha_{2}\geq 0
with equality if and only if

(\bm{\tau},\Pi^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
. Finally, we have

\begin{split}{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})&\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]\\
&=N^{2(1-\#\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime})-nD-d(\bm{\tau},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})}\Biggl(\Gamma\Bigl[\Bigl(\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\Pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}\Bigr)_{[n]},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]+o(1)\Biggr).\end{split}

We thus get that the product
\Phi_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\rho}}[B]\Phi^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau}}[\bm{T},\bar{\bm{T}}]{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}\Bigl[\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}\bm{\tau}\bm{\sigma}^{-1}\Bigr]
is of order

N^{2(1+\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})+\#(\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)-\#\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime})-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\tau})+n-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}}.

We notice that since
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\tau})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}
,

\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau})\leq(\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))_{[n]}
.
Hence, we can introduce the quantity

\begin{split}-2L\Bigl(\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1}),&(\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta));\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})\Bigr)\\
&=2\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})+2\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)-2K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\tau}^{-1})-2\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}).\end{split}

This quantity is non-positive, so that the maximum exponent of
N
for fixed
\eta
is

n+2(1-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta).

This is obtained whenever

\Bigl((\bm{\rho},\pi^{\prime}),(\bm{\tau},\Pi^{\prime\prime})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
. Note that by Remark
8.4
, this set is non-empty. The
maximal exponent is then obtained whenever
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
.
This gives the result.

In the case where
(T,\bar{T})
satisfies the scaling assumption
(
160
), the proof is identical, except that

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathcal{N}}(\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau})
instead of

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\tau})
.
∎

8.2
Free cumulants of products of tensors

We now compute the free cumulants of products of tensors in the case
of a product of mixed tensors, and then of a product of a mixed and
pure tensor.

Theorem 8.9
.

Let
A
be a random LU-invariant mixed tensor, and
B
be a random
mixed tensor independent from
A
. Assume that
A
and
B
satisfy the scaling assumption (
128
). For all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, we have

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot A]=N^{2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}-nD}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)+o(1)\Bigr),

where

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}=1_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\\
\bigl((\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\rho},\pi_{2})\bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{2},\bm{\rho}}(a).

Remark 8.10

(Mixed connected case)
.

In the mixed case, when
K(\bm{\sigma})=1
, the definition of
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
simplifies. Indeed, the condition

L\Bigl(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}),\pi_{2}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Bigr)=0

immediately implies that

\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
. Together with the
fact that

(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
,
and thus that
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi_{1}
, we
get that
\pi_{1}=\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
. Hence, if

K(\bm{\sigma})=1

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi,\bm{\rho}}(a).

We now notice that if
(\bm{\rho},\pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
then we
immediately get that
\pi=\Pi(\bm{\rho})
. We finally get a
formula analogous to the one of Theorem
8.1
: if

K(\bm{\sigma})=1
,

\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot a)=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathbb{G}(\bm{\sigma})}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\rho}),\bm{\rho}}(a).

Proof.

By Proposition
7.4
, we have

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot A]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
