\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[B]\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[A].

Using Theorem
4.4
, we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot A]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n}\end{subarray}}N^{2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}-K(\bm{\rho})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+\#\bm{\rho}-nD}\\
&\times\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}(a)+o(1)\Bigr).\end{split}

The proof is the very similar to the end of the one of Proposition

8.5
. We use Lemma
4.5

to get

\begin{split}2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}-K(\bm{\rho})-&K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+\#\bm{\rho}-nD\\
&\leq 2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}-nD,\end{split}

with equality if and only if
(\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
. We then introduce the quantity
L
defined in (
13
):

-2L\Bigl(\pi^{\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Bigr)=2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})-\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma})).

Since
\pi^{\prime}\vee\pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n}
, we get

2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\pi^{\prime\prime}-K(\bm{\rho})-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+\#\bm{\rho}-nD\leq 2(1-K(\bm{\sigma}))+\#\bm{\sigma}-nD,

with equality if and only if

(\pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma})
and

L\Bigl(\pi^{\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma});\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Bigr)=0
.

We then notice that this is equivalent to having

\Bigl((\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi^{\prime}),(\bm{\sigma},\pi^{\prime\prime})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}).

This gives the claim.
∎

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
(T,\bar{T})
be a random LU-invariant pure tensor, and
B
be
a random mixed tensor independent from
(T,\bar{T})
. Assume that

(T,\bar{T})
satisfy the scaling assumption
(
165
) and that
A
satisfies
(
128
). For all
n\in\mathbf{N}^{*}
and

\bm{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}
, we have

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot T,\bar{T}]=N^{n(1-D)+2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})}\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})\Bigr)

where

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi_{1}\in\mathcal{P}(n),\Pi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\pi_{1}\vee\Pi_{2}=1_{n,\bar{n}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}\\
\bigl((\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi_{1}),(\bm{\rho},\Pi_{2})\bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi_{1},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi_{2},\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t}).

Remark 8.12

(Connected case)
.

In the pure case, when
K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})=1
, the definition
of
\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
simplifies as in the mixed case. Indeed, for all
\eta\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
,
the condition

L\Bigl(\pi_{1}\vee\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}),(\Pi_{2}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))_{[n]};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Bigr)=0

immediately implies that

\pi_{1}\leq\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
. Together with the fact
that

(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi_{1})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm m}}(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
,
which implies
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi_{1}
, we get
that
\pi_{1}=\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})
. Thus, if

K(\bm{\sigma})=1

\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(b\cdot t,\bar{t})=\sum_{(\bm{\rho},\Pi)\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})}\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}),\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi,\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t}).

Proof.

By Proposition
7.4
, we have

\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\Pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n,\bar{n}}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}[B]\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}[A].

Using Theorem
4.4
and Theorem
5.12
, we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{{\rm p}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[B\cdot T,\bar{T}]=\sum_{\bm{\rho}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{D}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\rm p}}(n)\\
\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho})\leq\pi^{\prime\prime}\\
\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n}\end{subarray}}N^{2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n(1-D)-\varepsilon(\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho})}\\
&\quad\times\Bigl(\kappa^{{\rm m}}_{\pi^{\prime},\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}}(b)\kappa^{{\rm p}}_{\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho}}(t,\bar{t})+o(1)\Bigr).\end{split}

Let
\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\Pi^{\prime\prime},\bm{\rho})
. Using Lemma
5.13
, the exponent of
N
satisfies

\begin{split}2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}-&K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n(1-D)-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)\\
&\leq 2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})-K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta))-d(\bm{\sigma},\eta)+n(1-D),\end{split}

with equality if and only
(\bm{\rho},\Pi^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
.

We then introduce the quantity
L
defined in (
13
):

-2L\Biggl(\pi^{\prime},\Bigr(\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigl)_{[n]};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Biggr)=2\#+2\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)-2-2K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})

where we used that
\#\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=\#1_{n,\bar{n}}=1
, since
in the sum above we only consider partitions that satisfy

\pi^{\prime}\vee\Pi^{\prime\prime}=1_{n,\bar{n}}
. Hence, we get

\begin{split}2(\#\pi^{\prime}+\#\Pi^{\prime\prime}-&K_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\rho},\eta)-K(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1}))-d(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\rho})+n(1-D)-d(\bm{\rho},\eta)\\
&\leq 2-\varepsilon(\bm{\sigma})+n(1-D)\end{split}

with equality if and only if

•

(\bm{\rho},\Pi^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)
;

•

L\Biggl(\pi^{\prime},\Bigr(\Pi^{\prime\prime}\vee\Pi_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma},\eta)\Bigl)_{[n]};\Pi(\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1})\Biggr)=0
;

•

\eta\in\mathsf{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma})
.

These conditions are equivalent to having

\Bigl((\bm{\sigma}\bm{\rho}^{-1},\pi^{\prime}),(\bm{\rho},\Pi^{\prime\prime})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{I}\mathfrak{S}_{{\rm p}}(\bm{\sigma}).

This entails the result.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
2.14

By the definition (
50
), we have

{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}[\pi,{\bm{\nu}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{B\in\pi^{\prime}}{\rm W}_{N}(\bm{\nu}{{}_{|_{B}}}).

It was shown by Novak
[
Nov10
, Theorem 3.1]
that the Weingarten function
admits an expansion in powers of
1/N
as
N\to\infty

1/N
in
terms of monotone walks: for all
\rho\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
and
1\leq c\leq D

{\rm W}_{N}(\rho)=N^{-n}\sum_{r\geq 0}\frac{\#\mathsf{w}^{r}(\rho)}{N^{r}}.

We get by exchanging the sums,

{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}[\pi,{\bm{\nu}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi\leq\pi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\{r_{c,B}\colon 1\leq c\leq D,B\in\pi^{\prime}\}}(-1)^{\sum_{i}r_{c,B}}N^{-n-\sum_{i}r_{c,B}}\mu_{\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{B\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\#\mathsf{w}^{r_{c,B}}(\nu{{}_{|_{B}}}).

Introduce the sets

\mathsf{w}^{r}(\pi,\nu)=\Bigl\{\bm{\tau}\in\mathsf{w}^{r}(\nu)\colon\Pi(\nu)\vee\Pi(\bm{\tau})=\pi\Bigr\}.

We have

\begin{split}{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}&[\pi,{\bm{\nu}}]\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime},\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{D}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi\vee\pi_{1}\vee\cdots\vee\pi_{D}\leq\pi^{\prime}\\
\forall c,\Pi(\nu_{c})\leq\pi_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\{r_{c,B}\colon 1\leq c\leq D,B\in\pi^{\prime}\}}(-1)^{\sum_{i}r_{c,B}}N^{-n-\sum_{i}r_{c,B}}\mu_{\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{B\in\pi_{c}}\#\mathsf{w}^{r_{c,B}}(1_{B},\nu{{}_{|_{B}}}).\end{split}

The Moebius formula for partitions (
16
) then gives

{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}[\pi,{\bm{\nu}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{D}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi\vee\pi_{1}\vee\cdots\pi_{D}=1_{n}\\
\forall c,\Pi(\nu_{c})\leq\pi_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\{r_{c,B}\colon 1\leq c\leq D,B\in\pi_{c}\}}(-1)^{\sum_{c,B}r_{c,B}}N^{-n-\sum_{c,B}r_{c,B}}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{B\in\pi_{c}}\#\mathsf{w}^{r_{c,B}}(1_{B},\nu{{}_{|_{B}}}).

By Remark
2.13
, the exponent of
N
is maximal
whenever for all
1\leq c\leq D
and
B\in\pi_{c}
,
r_{c,B}=2(\#B-1)-|\nu_{c}{{}_{|_{B}}}|
. This gives
the exponent of
N

2(\sum_{c}\#\pi_{c}-nD)-\sum_{c}\#\nu_{c}.

Finally, we introduce the quantity
L_{D}
defined in (
14
):

-2L_{D}\Bigl(\{\pi_{c}\},\pi^{\prime};\{\Pi(\nu_{c})\}\Bigr)=2\sum_{c=1}^{D}\Bigl(\#\pi_{c}-\#\nu_{c}\Bigr)-2(1-\#\pi^{\prime}).

Hence, we have

2\Bigl(\sum_{c}\#\pi_{c}-nD\Bigr)-\sum_{c}\#\nu_{c}\leq\sum_{c}\#\nu_{c}-2nD+2(1-\#\pi^{\prime})

with equality if and only if

L_{D}\Bigl(\{\pi_{c}\},\pi^{\prime};\{\Pi(\nu_{c})\}\Bigr)=0
. We end up with

{\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}[\pi,{\bm{\nu}}]=N^{2(1-\#\pi^{\prime})+\sum_{c}\#\nu_{c}-2nD}\Biggl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{D}\in\mathcal{P}(n)\\
\pi\vee\pi_{1}\vee\cdots\vee\pi_{D}=1_{n}\\
\forall c,\Pi(\nu_{c})\leq\pi_{c}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{d(\bm{\nu})}\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{B\in\pi_{c}}\gamma(\nu{{}_{|_{B}}})+\order{N^{-1}}\Biggr),

as wanted.

To treat the pure case, it suffices to use Remark
2.11
which gives

{\rm W}^{{\rm p}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi,{\bm{\nu}}]={\rm W}^{{\rm m}}_{{\rm C},N}[\Pi_{[n]},{\bm{\nu}}]=N^{2(1-\#\Pi)+\sum_{c}\#\nu_{c}-2nD}\Bigl(\Gamma[\Pi_{[n]},\bm{\nu}]+\order{N^{-1}}\Bigr).\qed
